    cs.CL cs.AI cs.IR

    Scoring-Rule Sensitivity in LLM Evaluation: Evidence from Indian Financial Regulatory Text

    Authors: Rajveer Singh Pall

    Abstract: …the verdicts of a cross-model judge sharing no model family with any evaluated system - plus one operational composite regime. The two primary regimes show no positive rank correspondence (Spearman rho = -0.273, p = 0.39, not significant), and no model holds its rank across all three regimes. Strict accuracy spans 75.12-89.66%; judge-only accuracy spans 87.1… ▽ More A benchmark leaderboard reports one accuracy number, but that number is a joint product of a model and a scoring rule. We ask what changes when only the rule changes, and find that model ordering is highly sensitive to it. IndiaFinBench is a 406-item expert-annotated benchmark over SEBI and RBI regulatory text covering regulatory interpretation, numerical reasoning, contradiction detection, and temporal reasoning. We score twelve LLMs zero-shot under two primary regimes over the same predictions - strict four-stage string matching, and the verdicts of a cross-model judge sharing no model family with any evaluated system - plus one operational composite regime. The two primary regimes show no positive rank correspondence (Spearman rho = -0.273, p = 0.39, not significant), and no model holds its rank across all three regimes. Strict accuracy spans 75.12-89.66%; judge-only accuracy spans 87.19-94.83% with a different leader and trailer; the composite compresses the field to 94.1-98.0%, under which DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Llama-70B ties for first despite ranking last under strict scoring, because the judge reclassifies 93.9% of its strict errors as correct. Output verbosity does not explain the reclassification once task and model composition are adjusted for, a negative result we report as such. Item-level analysis compounds the finding: 148 of 406 items (36.5%) are answered identically by all twelve models and separate no pair of systems, while a predefined higher-discrimination band captures 134 items. We also anchor the scale against one non-specialist evaluator, who scored 80.0% (48/60; 95% Wilson CI [68.2, 88.2]) on a 60-item medium/hard subset; paired bootstrap tests find no significant difference against any model after multiplicity correction. Data, harness, judge verdicts, predictions: https://github.com/Rajveer-code/IndiaFinBench △ Less

    Submitted 26 October, 2026; v1 submitted 21 April, 2026; originally announced April 2026.

    Comments: 35 pages, 7 figures, 15 tables. Substantially revised from v2 (new title, scoring-rule analysis). Dataset, evaluation harness, and predictions at https://github.com/Rajveer-code/IndiaFinBench

    MSC Class: 68T50 ACM Class: I.2.7; I.2.6; H.3.1

    math.OC

    Proximal Point Newton Methods for Nonconvex Optimization

    Authors: Chang He, Shuzhong Zhang

    Abstract: Tensor methods offer improved complexity guarantees for nonconvex optimization, but evaluating high-order derivatives and solving the corresponding subproblems can be computationally expensive. In this paper, we develop a proximal point Newton framework that exploits third-derivative smoothness using only gradients and Hessians. Under Hessian Lipschitz conti… ▽ More Tensor methods offer improved complexity guarantees for nonconvex optimization, but evaluating high-order derivatives and solving the corresponding subproblems can be computationally expensive. In this paper, we develop a proximal point Newton framework that exploits third-derivative smoothness using only gradients and Hessians. Under Hessian Lipschitz continuity, a single-loop proximal point Newton method that alternates between negative-curvature directions and Newton steps finds an $(\varepsilon_g,\varepsilon_H)$-approximate second-order stationary point within $\mathcal{O}(\max\{\varepsilon_g^{-3/2},\varepsilon_H^{-3}\})$ second-order oracle evaluations. When the third derivative is also Lipschitz continuous, we introduce an accelerated proximal point Newton method and obtain a dimension-free complexity of $\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\max\{\varepsilon_g^{-4/3},\varepsilon_H^{-2}\})$, matching that of the third-order tensor methods up to logarithmic factors without constructing tensors or solving tensor subproblems. We further develop an adaptive inexact Newton--CG variant that requires no prior knowledge of Lipschitz constants and preserves the improved oracle complexity with high probability. Under uniformly bounded queried Hessians, this variant achieves a Hessian-vector product complexity of $\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\max\{\varepsilon_g^{-5/3},\varepsilon_H^{-5/2}\})$, improving upon the previous $\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\max\{\varepsilon_g^{-3}\varepsilon_H^{5/2},\varepsilon_H^{-7/2}\})$ result attained by existing second-order methods such as cubic regularized Newton method and Newton--CG method. Preliminary numerical experiments are encouraging. △ Less

    Submitted 26 October, 2026; originally announced October 2026.

    cs.CV cs.MM

    Hierarchical Multimodal Interaction Reasoning for Dyadic Relationship Recognition

    Authors: Wang Tang, Fethiye Irmak Dogan, Linbo Qing, Ce Zhu, Hatice Gunes

    Abstract: …objective, overlooking that relationships may rely on different interaction patterns. For instance, distant relationships can often be inferred from direct behavioural correspondence, whereas close relationships are better reflected by coordinated affective, behavioural, and multimodal responses. To address this limitation, we propose HiRe-DRR, a Hierarchica… ▽ More Dyadic relationship recognition aims to identify the latent social relationship between two individuals from their multimodal interactions. Existing methods typically project heterogeneous cues into a shared representation and optimise a flat classification objective, overlooking that relationships may rely on different interaction patterns. For instance, distant relationships can often be inferred from direct behavioural correspondence, whereas close relationships are better reflected by coordinated affective, behavioural, and multimodal responses. To address this limitation, we propose HiRe-DRR, a Hierarchical Multimodal Interaction Reasoning framework that organises relationship evidence from three complementary perspectives: feature level, interaction type, and relationship hierarchy. HiRe-DRR decomposes facial, bodily, acoustic, and textual cues into low- and high-level representations and constructs homogeneous and heterogeneous cross-participant interactions. We further introduce the Mixture-of-Interaction Transformer (MoI-Former), which treats diverse interactions as specialised experts and employs relation-aware sparse routing to identify relationship-relevant evidence while preserving contextual interaction information. The proposed Hierarchical Conditional Loss (HC-Loss) exploits the hierarchical nature of social relationships through coarse-to-fine supervision, enabling structured relationship reasoning. Extensive experiments on three dyadic relationship benchmarks demonstrate that HiRe-DRR outperforms recent representative image-, video-, and multimodal methods. Our routing analysis further reveals that distant relationships tend to rely on low-level homogeneous interactions, whereas closer relationships focus on high-level heterogeneous interactions. Our code is publicly available at: https://github.com/tw-repository/HiRe-DRR. △ Less

    astro-ph.GA

    Impact of Gaia-Sausage-Enceladus Debris on Milky Way Escape-velocity Estimates in TNG50

    Authors: Yin Wu, Haining Li, Shi Shao, Xiang-Xiang Xue, Lan Zhang, Gang Zhao

    Abstract: …underestimation of the escape velocity corresponds to an $\sim 6\%$ underestimation of the total mass. This improvement persists in LAMOST-like magnitude and footprint selection. GSE-like debris is therefore a key contributor to the systematic underestimation of escape-velocity measurements, with direct implications for the inferred MW mass. ▽ More The escape velocity is a key probe of the Milky Way (MW) potential, but standard estimates can be biased by unrelaxed substructures. Using MW analogs from the TNG50 cosmological simulation, we investigate the impact of the dominant, highly radial Gaia-Sausage-Enceladus (GSE) debris on escape-velocity measurements. We introduce an adaptive selection strategy that balances statistical reliability with sample size to identify the power-law decline regime of the local halo velocity distribution. Applying this strategy to 16 MW analogs with prominent GSE-like structures and continuous high-velocity tails, we find that retaining GSE-like stars yields a systematic underestimate, with a recovery fraction of $97.5^{+2.1}_{-2.9}\%$, while removing GSE-like stars alone increases the recovery fraction to $99.4^{+2.1}_{-2.5}\%$. The $\sim 2\%$ underestimation of the escape velocity corresponds to an $\sim 6\%$ underestimation of the total mass. This improvement persists in LAMOST-like magnitude and footprint selection. GSE-like debris is therefore a key contributor to the systematic underestimation of escape-velocity measurements, with direct implications for the inferred MW mass. △ Less

    econ.TH

    Optimally Selling Heterogeneous Objects by Quantity

    Authors: Xun Chen

    Abstract: …ex ante symmetry, assuming free disposal, independence between the quantity-value vector and the vector of bundle shortfalls from their respective quantity values, and corresponding distributional and support conditions. For an arbitrary number of objects, applications yield explicit optimal menus that involve quantity discounts, sparse quantity options, or… ▽ More I study the optimality of selling K heterogeneous objects to a buyer with private values through a quantity menu: the seller posts a price for each number of objects, and the buyer chooses a quantity, pays its price, and selects any objects up to that quantity. I propose a pricing method that aggregates critical values for second differences of quantity values (the highest values attainable at each quantity). Critical values can be determined separately when each second difference's conditional inverse-hazard ratio depends only on that difference. For additive values, I show that the resulting menu is optimal among all incentive-compatible and individually rational mechanisms under explicit distributional and support conditions, including ex ante symmetry across object labels. A separate result applies the same pricing method to obtain an optimal menu for nonadditive bundle values without ex ante symmetry, assuming free disposal, independence between the quantity-value vector and the vector of bundle shortfalls from their respective quantity values, and corresponding distributional and support conditions. For an arbitrary number of objects, applications yield explicit optimal menus that involve quantity discounts, sparse quantity options, or two-part tariffs. These results differ from those establishing the optimality of pure bundling in Haghpanah and Hartline (2021), nested bundling in Yang (2025), separate sales in Kwak (2026), and core-peripheral bundling in Zhong (2026). △ Less

    The Keller problem for the lowest positive eigenvalue of Dirac operators with Lorentz scalar potentials

    Authors: David Gontier, Hanne Van Den Bosch

    Abstract: …norm. We prove that optimal potentials always exist and that the corresponding eigenspinors solve the nonlinear Soler equation. This provides a variational proof of existence of solitary waves for this equation. We prove that there are no zero modes for such operators in dimensions $1$ and $2$, and conjecture that zero mode may exist in dimension $3$. In di… ▽ More We study the Keller problem for the Dirac operator $\mathcal{D}_m - Vβ$ in $\mathbb{R}^d$, and minimize its lowest positive eigenvalue over all potentials $V$ with prescribed $L^p$ norm. We prove that optimal potentials always exist and that the corresponding eigenspinors solve the nonlinear Soler equation. This provides a variational proof of existence of solitary waves for this equation. We prove that there are no zero modes for such operators in dimensions $1$ and $2$, and conjecture that zero mode may exist in dimension $3$. In dimension $d = 2$, we provide numerical evidence that the optimal potentials concentrate on flower-shape regions, which are not necessarily radial, showing that Faber--Krahn type results may fail for such Dirac operators. In dimension $1$, all quantities are explicit. △ Less

    math.SG math.DG math.DS math.SP

    Contact topology and intrinsic characterization of transversely vanishing curl eigenforms

    Authors: Robert Cardona, Willi Kepplinger

    Abstract: …-forms which are curl eigenforms for some smooth Riemannian metric. We deduce a generalization of the classical correspondence, due to Etnyre and Ghrist, between Reeb-like fields of contact forms and nowhere-vanishing rotational Beltrami fields to the setting of transversely vanishing vector fields. ▽ More We study the geometry and topology of transversely vanishing eigenforms of the curl operator on $3$-manifolds, placing them in the broader framework of near-contact forms. We prove a Gray-type stability theorem for the kernels of near-contact forms, which we call near-contact structures, and derive a local neighborhood theorem around their singularities. As applications, we obtain a stability result for the contact topology of curl eigenforms, a classification of near-contact structures in terms of formal homotopical data, and a proof of the existence of contact structures on closed orientable 3-manifolds based on the spectral theory of the curl operator. We also study the symplectization of curl eigenforms as a possible bridge to symplectic dynamics. Finally, we characterize those transversely vanishing $1$-forms which are curl eigenforms for some smooth Riemannian metric. We deduce a generalization of the classical correspondence, due to Etnyre and Ghrist, between Reeb-like fields of contact forms and nowhere-vanishing rotational Beltrami fields to the setting of transversely vanishing vector fields. △ Less

    physics.flu-dyn

    A Kinetic Study of the Interactions of an Acoustic Vibrating Surface with Hypersonic Boundary-Layer Instabilities

    Authors: Mert Senkardesler, Irmak T. Karpuzcu, Deborah A. Levin

    Abstract: …waves at other frequencies and the mean flow essentially unaltered. The ability of the kinetic simulations to capture and describe linear perturbations at Reynolds numbers corresponding to those of Mach 6 flight at altitudes of $\sim 20$ km suggests that DSMC provides a theoretical understanding of controlling laminar-turbulent transition through AVS materia… ▽ More The response of Mack mode instabilities to a simple acoustic vibrating surface (AVS) is studied using the Direct Simulation Monte Carlo (DSMC) method to capture and analyze spontaneous second-mode instability growth. The DSMC simulations, analyzed with the Dynamic Mode Decomposition (DMD) method, model flow over a flat plate at an unseeded freestream Mach number of 6 and unit Reynolds number $Re_1\sim 1.18\times10^7$ m$^{-1}$. Power spectral density (PSD) analysis was found to identify the dominant frequencies of 200 to 500 kHz, in line with linear stability theory (LST) predictions. Closed-form kinetic-theory expressions were derived to predict the amplitude of the DSMC fluctuations in the window-averaged snapshots. It was observed that the DSMC-simulated flowfield naturally developed second Mack mode instabilities without any external seeding, i.e., arising spontaneously from the kinetic fluctuations of the simulated gas and of amplitude higher than the predicted DSMC fluctuations. Targeted interaction with these flow instabilities is demonstrated for an AVS, where forcing at the unstable frequency of 250 kHz results in amplified waves downstream, while at the stable frequency of 400 kHz AVS-induced disturbances are damped. The AVS thus excites only the waves at its forcing frequency, leaving the instability waves at other frequencies and the mean flow essentially unaltered. The ability of the kinetic simulations to capture and describe linear perturbations at Reynolds numbers corresponding to those of Mach 6 flight at altitudes of $\sim 20$ km suggests that DSMC provides a theoretical understanding of controlling laminar-turbulent transition through AVS materials in hypersonic boundary layers. △ Less

    Comments: 49 pages, 20 figures; main text (31 pages, 9 figures) followed by the Supplemental Material (18 pages, 11 figures). Revised and shortened version resubmitted to Physical Review Fluids; new title

    cs.CV cs.GR

    Token-to-Token Alignment of Text Embeddings for Semantic Blending

    Authors: Saar Huberman, Ron Mokady, Or Patashnik, Daniel Cohen-Or

    Abstract: …generated from sequences of tokens derived from these prompts. However, the space of token sequences lacks a consistent accessible structure: semantically similar images may correspond to sequences that differ in wording, ordering, and placement of concepts, while similar token sequences may encode very different semantics. This apparent lack of structure ma… ▽ More In modern generative models, images are specified and controlled through text prompts. In practice, images are generated from sequences of tokens derived from these prompts. However, the space of token sequences lacks a consistent accessible structure: semantically similar images may correspond to sequences that differ in wording, ordering, and placement of concepts, while similar token sequences may encode very different semantics. This apparent lack of structure makes it difficult to perform smooth transitions in this space, hindering applications such as image blending and continuous control of edits. We argue that this limitation stems not from the absence of semantic structure, but from misalignment between representations. To address this misalignment, we introduce Token-to-Token alignment, a framework that establishes explicit semantic correspondence between tokens across prompts. Our approach transforms prompts into a structured representation in which semantically corresponding concepts are mapped to consistent positions across prompts, and then aligns their token embeddings based on semantic similarity. Concretely, the method consists of two stages: a structural alignment that rephrases prompts into a shared structured form, followed by an embedding-level alignment that matches token representations across prompts. With this alignment in place, simple linear interpolation becomes a meaningful operation, producing smooth and coherent semantic transitions and enabling applications such as blending and continuous editing. Our results show that text embedding spaces in text-to-image models implicitly encode a continuous semantic structure that becomes accessible once representations are properly aligned, suggesting that semantic control can be achieved by organizing existing representations rather than modifying the generative model. △ Less

    Submitted 26 October, 2026; v1 submitted 22 June, 2026; originally announced June 2026.

    Comments: Accepted to NeurIPS 2026 (Spotlight). Project page: https://saarhuberman.github.io/token-to-token/

    A GOOFy Three Higgs Doublet Model

    Authors: I. de Medeiros Varzielas, P. M. Ferreira, S. Oliveira

    Abstract: …symmetry including extensions to the fermion sector. We prove renormalization group invariance through a direct calculation of the model's beta functions and analyse the corresponding phenomenology. The model is shown to be compatible with current LHC observations, displaying interesting features which may be probed in the near future. ▽ More Recent developments in two-Higgs doublet models identified regions of parameter space which are renormalization-group invariant to all orders of perturbation theory but cannot be described by any of the known symmetries of those models. These were dubbed GOOFy symmetries, as they involve scalar doublets and their complex conjugates transforming independently, and imaginary scaling of spacetime coordinates is needed to ensure invariance of kinetic terms. We build a three-Higgs doublet model with a GOOFy symmetry including extensions to the fermion sector. We prove renormalization group invariance through a direct calculation of the model's beta functions and analyse the corresponding phenomenology. The model is shown to be compatible with current LHC observations, displaying interesting features which may be probed in the near future. △ Less

    Asymmetric Reheating of a Dark Sector

    Authors: Catarina Cosme, João G. Rosa, Inês Sequeira

    Abstract: …in a framework with two sequestered MSSM-like sectors (the ``visible'' and ``dark'' sectors) with different supersymmetry-breaking scales, with dark matter corresponding to the lightest dark baryons. In this setup, independent Affleck-Dine dynamics in the two sectors offers a natural explanation for the comparable abundances of visible and da… ▽ More We study asymmetric post-inflationary reheating in a framework with two sequestered MSSM-like sectors (the ``visible'' and ``dark'' sectors) with different supersymmetry-breaking scales, with dark matter corresponding to the lightest dark baryons. In this setup, independent Affleck-Dine dynamics in the two sectors offers a natural explanation for the comparable abundances of visible and dark matter. We relate the resulting temperature hierarchy to the inflaton's partial decay widths in different kinematical regimes, where it may decay into dark sector particles via 2-, 3- or 4-body processes. The latter case yields the most asymmetric reheating scenario, and we discuss the implications of this regime for the dark sector's thermal history, namely regarding the corresponding strong and weak interactions. While in this kinematical regime the contribution of light dark sector degrees of freedom to $ΔN_{\rm eff}$ is negligible, we show that it increases significantly in the regime where 3-body decays are possible, particularly near the threshold for dark Higgs pair-production where $ΔN_{\rm eff}^{\max} \simeq 0.078$. The current combined BBN+CMB+BAO bound leaves only a narrow allowed interval of the 2-body decay regime immediately above threshold, while next-generation CMB experiments could probe slightly below it. △ Less

    physics.chem-ph

    RPAx Instabilities from Ground- and Excited-State References

    Authors: Loris Burth, Marios-Petros Kitsaras, Pierre-François Loos

    Abstract: Random-phase approximation with exchange (RPAx) calculations based on ground- or excited-state references can yield complex excitation energies, causing the corresponding RPAx correlation energy to become complex as well. In this work, we investigate the origin of these complex energies using \ce{H2} in the 6-31G basis set as a minimal model. For the ground-… ▽ More Random-phase approximation with exchange (RPAx) calculations based on ground- or excited-state references can yield complex excitation energies, causing the corresponding RPAx correlation energy to become complex as well. In this work, we investigate the origin of these complex energies using \ce{H2} in the 6-31G basis set as a minimal model. For the ground-state restricted Hartree-Fock reference, complex roots emerge at the well-known triplet instability associated with the appearance of a lower-lying unrestricted, symmetry-broken solution. For excited-state references, we identify two distinct mechanisms leading to complex excitation energies. The first is similarly associated with symmetry breaking. The second arises when a positive-energy excitation becomes degenerate with the positive-energy de-excitation associated with a negative-energy transition. Finally, we demonstrate the practical implications of these findings for spin-flip Bethe-Salpeter equation calculations of cyclobutadiene and state-specific core-ionization calculations for selected systems. These examples show that, in the absence of the relevant instabilities, methods sharing the generalized eigenvalue structure of RPAx can be applied to excited-state references without resorting to the Tamm-Dancoff or core-valence separation approximations, while retaining real excitation energies. △ Less

    cond-mat.mtrl-sci cond-mat.str-el

    Anisotropic Structural and Magnetic Response in Sm$_2$Ir$_8$Al$_{31}$: Al-Sublattice Reconstruction upon Sm Substitution

    Authors: Mingyu Xu, Weiwei Xie

    Abstract: …. Magnetic susceptibility measurements reveal a direction dependence, including a broad anomaly near 50 K for selected magnetic-field orientations. However, the absence of corresponding anomalies in heat capacity, electrical resistivity, and lattice parameters suggests that this feature is more consistent with an anisotropic magnetic crossover than a bulk th… ▽ More Chemical pressure plays a critical role in determining the crystal structures and physical properties of intermetallic compounds. Here, we report the synthesis, crystal structure, and physical properties of Sm$_2$Ir$_8$Al$_{31}$, a structural analog of UIr$_4$Al$_{15}$, in which the smaller 5f uranium atoms are replaced by larger 4f samarium atoms. Single-crystal X-ray diffraction reveals a tetragonal $\sqrt{2}\times\sqrt{2}\times1$ superstructure with space group $P\bar{4}2_1m$, derived from a UIr$_4$Al$_{15}$-like average structure with space group $P4_2/nmc$. Comparison with UIr$_4$Al$_{15}$ shows that chemical pressure from U-to-Sm substitution produces a strongly anisotropic structural response rather than a uniform expansion of the Ir-Al framework. While one Ir coordination environment undergoes only modest changes, the other exhibits substantial local rearrangement. The Al sublattice primarily accommodates these structural distortions, with selected Al sites showing positional splitting, anisotropic atomic displacement ellipsoids, and partial occupancies, resulting in an Al-rich composition of approximately Sm$_2$Ir$_8$Al$_{31}$. Magnetic susceptibility measurements reveal a direction dependence, including a broad anomaly near 50 K for selected magnetic-field orientations. However, the absence of corresponding anomalies in heat capacity, electrical resistivity, and lattice parameters suggests that this feature is more consistent with an anisotropic magnetic crossover than a bulk thermodynamic phase transition. These results highlight how chemical substitution at the f-element site can induce Al-sublattice reconstruction and modify the magnetic response within a closely related Ir-Al structural framework. △ Less

    When Connectivity Misleads: Guiding Agentic Search with Contrastive Evidence Paths

    Authors: Junchen Li, Liang Xu, Rongzheng Wang, Qizhi Chen, Junhao Wen, Shuang Liang

    Abstract: …these scores against the same graph's background visitation distribution to mitigate structural bias. CEP ranks candidates by the calibrated scores and retains the corresponding source paths. Building on CEP, we develop Structured Corpus Interaction (SCI), which organizes candidates and their connecting paths into a structured Map, enabling agents to ver… ▽ More Search agents can use cross-document graphs to discover sources through multi-hop connections and choose subsequent actions based on candidate rankings and relationships within a limited observation window. However, documents may rank highly because their numerous connections make them easier to reach, without necessarily providing more valuable evidence. Graph diffusion methods such as Personalized PageRank (PPR) aggregate propagation contributions from multiple sources, allowing highly connected distractor nodes to crowd the observation window. Flat node scores also obscure cross-document evidence trails, limiting continued exploration. We propose Contrastive Evidence Paths (CEP), which scores candidates by their strongest individual paths on a query-conditioned document graph and calibrates these scores against the same graph's background visitation distribution to mitigate structural bias. CEP ranks candidates by the calibrated scores and retains the corresponding source paths. Building on CEP, we develop Structured Corpus Interaction (SCI), which organizes candidates and their connecting paths into a structured Map, enabling agents to verify source documents and pursue missing information through expansion from anchor documents. Experiments on BrowseComp-Plus and TREC-COVID show that SCI achieves state-of-the-art answer accuracy and nDCG@10 with the lowest generation cost. Compared with the strongest baselines by task performance, maximum reductions in tool calls and total generation costs exceed 50% and 40%, respectively. Further analysis confirms that SCI with CEP suppresses ranking inflation induced by structural connections and improves gold recall and exploration efficiency. △ Less

    Abstract: …is eventually periodic, and that the corresponding Hankel determinant sequence $Δ^{(n+2)}$ is $2n(n+1)$-periodic if $n$ is even, $2n(n+1)$-antiperiodic if $n$ is odd, and that it takes only the values $-2,-1,0,1,2$. This confirms part of a former conjecture of ours and complements similar results we obtained for all $\ell$-shifts of $Φ_n$ with… ▽ More Let $Φ_n(q)$ denote the $q$-deformation (in the sense of Morier-Genoud & Ovsienko) of the metallic number $φ_n=\frac{n+\sqrt{n^2+4}}{2}$. This is a power series with integer coefficients. We prove that the Hankel continued fraction of the $(n+2)$-shifted series $Φ_n^{(n+2)}$ is eventually periodic, and that the corresponding Hankel determinant sequence $Δ^{(n+2)}$ is $2n(n+1)$-periodic if $n$ is even, $2n(n+1)$-antiperiodic if $n$ is odd, and that it takes only the values $-2,-1,0,1,2$. This confirms part of a former conjecture of ours and complements similar results we obtained for all $\ell$-shifts of $Φ_n$ with $0\le\ell\le n+1$. In addition, we formulate a new conjecture of quasi-polynomiality for higher shifts $\ell\ge n+3$. The determinant values arise as telescoping products of the partial numerators of the Hankel continued fraction. Most of the technically involved and lengthy verifications are carried out by a symbolic computation program, whose code is publicly available on GitHub for independent verification. △ Less

    MEL: Coordinate-Preserving EEG Tokenization for fMRI Translation

    Authors: Xiangyu Liu, Zeting Yan, Zhitong Yin, Boyang Li, Xi Zhang

    Abstract: …perceptron (MLP) readouts. The retrospective analysis includes 29 resting-state scans from 22 participants, participant-paired uncertainty estimates, aggregation and correspondence controls, leave-one-participant-out regression, and refitted models on an external Oddball cohort. Results: Morlet-Ridge achieved a mean scan-wise correlation of 0.508 across si… ▽ More Background and Objective: Predicting regional blood-oxygen-level-dependent (BOLD) activity from EEG provides a computational approach to studying electrophysiological-hemodynamic associations. However, changes in input representation, readout architecture, and ensembling can obscure the source of a prediction gain. We investigate the contribution of target-relative spectral estimation under matched readout conditions. Methods: MEL converts each preceding 16-second EEG history into calibrated lag-channel-frequency measurements. Four equal-dimensional representations are compared using matched Ridge and multilayer perceptron (MLP) readouts. The retrospective analysis includes 29 resting-state scans from 22 participants, participant-paired uncertainty estimates, aggregation and correspondence controls, leave-one-participant-out regression, and refitted models on an external Oddball cohort. Results: Morlet-Ridge achieved a mean scan-wise correlation of 0.508 across six regional targets and the global signal, compared with 0.432 for canonical Fourier features. The participant-paired mean difference was 0.069 (95% bootstrap confidence interval, 0.037-0.101), with improvements in 19 of 22 participants. The Morlet MLP prediction ensemble reached 0.491. Shared coordinate permutations preserved prediction after refitting, whereas sample-wise permutations degraded it. Canonical Fourier regression reached 0.377 under participant exclusion; external unseen-participant correlation was 0.026. Conclusions: Spectral estimation materially affected regional BOLD prediction without requiring additional nonlinear readout capacity. MEL provides an explicit measurement interface for controlled comparisons, but weak external results constrain its generalizability. Confirmation requires untouched participant-separated data and split-aware preprocessing. △ Less

The approach uses the external proxy to locate the target-shock direction in the reduced-form innovations and synthesize the candidat… ▽ More Proxy SVARs often rely on a single credible proxy, leaving proxy exogeneity untestable. Narrative arguments used to justify proxy validity imply a stronger form of exogeneity. We formalize the strong exogeneity assumption and propose a corresponding test based on internal synthetic proxies. The approach uses the external proxy to locate the target-shock direction in the reduced-form innovations and synthesize the candidate shock into an additional internal synthetic proxies, which allows to test the joint exogeneity of the original and internal synthetic proxy. We derive the large-sample theory for the ISP test and show that the identification strength is governed by a direct measure of the target shock's non-Gaussianity. We study weak identification under asymptotic Gaussianity and develop an identification-robust version of the test. Monte Carlo experiments demonstrate the favorable power of the test when the proxy has a low signal-to-noise ratio. Applications to monetary policy and oil supply news shocks find no evidence against the exogeneity of the benchmark proxies. △ Less

    Efficient regime boundary detection in discontinuous simulation models

    Authors: Federico Betti, Eleni Chatzi, Paolo Tiso

    Abstract: …detection instead enables localized modeling strategies by partitioning the parameter space into regions of qualitatively distinct behavior. In many cases, regime switching corresponds to the excursion set of a simulator output. When such a quantity is discontinuous or non-smooth, regression-based algorithms for level set estimation can struggle to correctly… ▽ More Many computational models exhibit multiple operating regimes separated by abrupt or non-smooth transitions as the input parameters vary. Such regime transitions complicate the construction of global parametric reduced-order models, often necessitating large training datasets to capture the distinct system behaviors. Accurate regime boundary detection instead enables localized modeling strategies by partitioning the parameter space into regions of qualitatively distinct behavior. In many cases, regime switching corresponds to the excursion set of a simulator output. When such a quantity is discontinuous or non-smooth, regression-based algorithms for level set estimation can struggle to correctly approximate the boundary. In this work, we show that solving a resulting binary classification problem can improve sample efficiency and we further propose a novel adaptive sampling strategy for support vector machines. Unlike uncertainty-based sampling schemes, the proposed approach uses a continuous search space and efficiently explores the currently estimated boundary in its tangential directions by gradient-based dynamic sampling. Because the decision function admits analytical gradients, this exploration incurs negligible computational overhead relative to the underlying model evaluations. Then, a space-filling subset of the generated samples is selected as the next batch to be queried. We demonstrate improved sample efficiency compared to existing active learning algorithms on analytical and numerical examples. The proposed method provides a sample-efficient framework for regime boundary detection in a broad class of discontinuous simulation-driven problems. △ Less

    A gaussian-process reconstruction of gravitational-wave friction from dark standard sirens

    Authors: Michele Mancarella, Viola De Renzis, Alessandro Agapito

    Abstract: …, with a Gaussian process (GP) and derive the corresponding GW-to-EM luminosity-distance ratio $Ξ(z)$ by integration, explicitly retaining the relation between the local friction modification and its accumulated effect on the observed distance, while enforcing a monotonic and invertible GW luminosity distance. We apply the method to the GWTC-5.0 catalog, joi… ▽ More Standard sirens provide a direct probe of the cosmic distance-redshift relation and therefore a powerful test of whether gravitational waves (GWs) propagate as predicted by general relativity on cosmological scales. In many extensions of general relativity, GW propagation is modified by an anomalous friction term, which alters the amplitude evolution and leads to a GW luminosity distance that differs from its electromagnetic counterpart. In this work, we develop a non-parametric reconstruction of this anomalous friction from dark standard sirens. We reconstruct its redshift dependence, encoded in the friction function $α_M(z)$, with a Gaussian process (GP) and derive the corresponding GW-to-EM luminosity-distance ratio $Ξ(z)$ by integration, explicitly retaining the relation between the local friction modification and its accumulated effect on the observed distance, while enforcing a monotonic and invertible GW luminosity distance. We apply the method to the GWTC-5.0 catalog, jointly inferring the source population, modified propagation, and background cosmological parameters. We find no evidence for departures from GR, with $α_M=0$ and $Ξ=1$ consistent with the posterior over the redshift range probed by the catalog. The result is stable under variations of the GP kernel, smoothness and amplitude priors, resolution prescription, and likelihood treatment. We further introduce function-space diagnostics for comparing flexible and parametric reconstructions, including prior calibration, prior-to-posterior contraction, common summaries of propagation-population degeneracies, and principal-component analysis. These tools make explicit which features are informed by the data and which are imposed by the function-space prior, providing a framework for interpretable non-parametric tests of gravitational-wave propagation. △ Less

    Authors: Christopher David Roberts

    Abstract: …which couples members via transformer self-attention across the ensemble dimension. In both cases, the results are sensitive to ensemble size and apparent gains in aCRPS can correspond to systematic unreliability characterized by over-dispersion. We introduce trajectory transformers as a proof-of-concept that ensemble-size independence can be achieved. This… ▽ More Fair scores reward ensemble forecast members that behave like samples from the same distribution as the verifying observations. They are therefore an attractive choice as loss functions to train data-driven ensemble forecasts or post-processing methods when large training ensembles are either unavailable or computationally prohibitive. The adjusted continuous ranked probability score (aCRPS) is a fair and ensemble-size-unbiased scoring rule when applied to ensembles with forecast members that are interpretable as conditionally independent draws from an underlying predictive distribution. However, distribution-aware post-processing methods that introduce structural dependency between members can violate this assumption, such that aCRPS no longer functions as a fair scoring rule. We demonstrate this effect using two approaches designed to minimize the expected aCRPS of a finite ensemble: (1) a linear member-by-member calibration, which couples members through a common dependency on the sample ensemble mean, and (2) a deep-learning method, which couples members via transformer self-attention across the ensemble dimension. In both cases, the results are sensitive to ensemble size and apparent gains in aCRPS can correspond to systematic unreliability characterized by over-dispersion. We introduce trajectory transformers as a proof-of-concept that ensemble-size independence can be achieved. This approach is an adaptation of the Post-processing Ensembles with Transformers (PoET) framework and applies self-attention over lead time while preserving the conditional independence required by aCRPS. When applied to weekly mean $T_{2m}$ forecasts from the ECMWF subseasonal forecasting system, this approach successfully reduces systematic model biases whilst also improving or maintaining forecast reliability regardless of the ensemble size used in training or real-time forecasts. △ Less

We carefully disting… ▽ More We study the set of $n$-qubit pure quantum states. Specifically, we consider their incarnation as density matrices in Bloch representation. Building on prior work, we define a family of quadratic functions in Pauli expectation values, and fully characterise all instances that result in state-independent purity constraints. The resulting family of pure-state invariants is surprisingly rich in structure, with strong ties to the geometry of symplectic polar spaces. After charting the general landscape, we define a sub-family of pure-state invariants that satisfies the dynamical constraint of being stable under state update. We prove that it corresponds with (different embeddings of) symplectic Lie algebras $\mathfrak{sp}(2^{n-1})\subset\mathfrak{su}(2^n)$, which admit a generating set of Pauli operators. We carefully distinguish the symplectic case from its orthogonal counterpart, using the Arf invariant of quadratic refinements of the symplectic form on the projective Pauli group, which we further identify with quaternionic structures on $\mathbb{C}^{2^n}$ that are both generated by and restricted to Pauli operators. As a consequence of this analysis, we reveal previously largely overlooked structure in the $n$-qubit Pauli group. We highlight this in a number of examples. We characterise all state-independent uncertainty relations in variance for Pauli observables, as well as all Pauli channels for which the output fidelity and output purity are state-independent. We further show that the orbits of the projective stabiliser groups of pure-state invariants, which for $n\geq2$ are proper subgroups of the Clifford group, form complex projective state $2$-designs, while their unitary lifts form symplectic $2$-designs. △ Less

    Authors: Frank Barrows, Touheed Atif, Yiğit Subaşı, Tyler Volkoff

    Abstract: …target either through an output clamping term or through a target-dependent cyclic condition on the clock register. We show that these costs are nonlinear functions of the compilation infi… ▽ More Equilibrium learning trains a physical system using observables of its relaxed states. We extend this principle to gate-based quantum circuits by embedding a parametrized circuit in a Feynman-Kitaev (FK) Hamiltonian and imposing the compilation target either through an output clamping term or through a target-dependent cyclic condition on the clock register. The ground state energies and the overlaps of the corresponding ground states with the free history state define faithful cost functions for unitary and state compilation. We show that these costs are nonlinear functions of the compilation infidelity and therefore reshape the optimization landscape. We show that starting from the free history state, quantum phase estimation can prepare the relevant ground states and estimate the cost functions with polynomial resources. In two-qubit benchmarks, optimization with the equilibrium cost functions reaches a fixed fidelity threshold in fewer sweeps than standard compilation infidelity cost function for many instances. The results demonstrate the potential for encoding quantum learning problems as computation of equilibrium observables. △ Less

The restriction is placed on each cohort's block… ▽ More HonestDiD sensitivity analysis (Rambachan and Roth, 2023) is usually applied to event-study coefficients aggregated across cohorts. Under staggered adoption, the treated cohorts contributing to these coefficients change with event time, as do their control groups with estimators that use not-yet-treated units. Therefore, the restriction on the aggregated coefficients and the corresponding inference does not have a clear interpretation. This paper proposes to do the HonestDiD sensitivity analysis on a cohort basis. The restriction is placed on each cohort's block bias, which captures its violation of parallel trends relative to the units untreated when it first adopts treatment. The restriction on the block bias maps to the restriction on the estimation bias through an invertible linear decomposition. In simulations, the proposed cohort inference keeps nominal coverage when parallel trend violations differ across cohorts, where the inference based on aggregated coefficients can fail. Applying the method to Callaway and Sant'Anna (2021) and Bailey and Goodman-Bacon (2015), I highlight the trade-off between interpretable imposed restrictions and the precision demands of cohort inference. △ Less

The computation reduces to two-dimensional Yang-Mills theory on $\mathbb{RP}^2$ twisted by charge conjugation and leads to a matrix integral of a Wishart-Laguerre type. In the planar limit, the exact result interpolates between the weak-coupling gauge-theory result, the strong-coupling supergravity prediction for the orientifold, and the worldsheet crosscap in the pp-wave limit. △ Less

    Authors: Federico Petrovich, Daniel Staros, Daniel N. Blaschke, Jian-Xin Zhu, M. Cerezo

Together, these examples show that symmetry resolution, mode entanglement, reduced-density-matrix correlations, and conserved-operator structure pro… ▽ More Symmetries of quantum systems provide a natural way to organize the eigenspaces of many-body Hamiltonians. When degeneracies exist, however, they play a more subtle role as different sets of symmetry operators can resolve the same energy subspace into states with very different correlation properties. We study this interplay using a sequence of FeO and Fe$_2$O$_2$ models. For a Hubbard model of FeO, the ground space is 16-fold degenerate. Resolving it by local magnetizations gives a basis of Slater determinants, while total-spin quantum numbers select entangled and non-Gaussian states from the same ground manifold. Adding density-density Hund interactions breaks the independent spin rotations and reduces the ground space to a spin-reversal pair with maximal magnetization. These states are extremely close to Slater determinants in their natural occupations, yet have finite two-body cumulants and conserved-operator algebras whose orbit dimensions differ substantially from the Slater values. Fe$_2$O$_2$ provides a larger example in which spatial mode entanglement can be substantial while fermionic non-Gaussianity remains weak, with the conserved-operator structure again distinguishing the state from the corresponding Slater transformation class. Together, these examples show that symmetry resolution, mode entanglement, reduced-density-matrix correlations, and conserved-operator structure provide complementary information about interacting and degenerate electronic ground states. △ Less

We study Bayesian inference from noisy bond measurements of an Ising model in its paramagnetic phase, with true inverse temperature $β$ and measurement-noise strength $Δ$, while inference assumes corresponding parameters $β_Q$ and $Δ_Q$. We further perform Monte Carlo simulations of the mapped model in three dimensions and establish a finite overconfident region between the informative and uninformative phases, consistent with our analytical prediction. Finally, we show that the true model parameters can be inferred directly from the measurement records, providing a natural route to calibrating the inference model. △ Less

    AlgebrasTQD: Computing Algebras and Interfaces in Twisted Quantum Doubles

    Authors: Alison Warman

    Abstract: We present AlgebrasTQD, an open-source code written in GAP (Groups, Algorithms, and Programming), for computing condensable algebras and the corresponding topological boundaries and interfaces of twisted quantum doubles $D^ω(G)$. These algebras can be used to classify 1+1d gapped and gapless phases whose symmetry is related by gauging to a finite group $G$ w… ▽ More We present AlgebrasTQD, an open-source code written in GAP (Groups, Algorithms, and Programming), for computing condensable algebras and the corresponding topological boundaries and interfaces of twisted quantum doubles $D^ω(G)$. These algebras can be used to classify 1+1d gapped and gapless phases whose symmetry is related by gauging to a finite group $G$ with anomaly $ω\in H^3(G,U(1))$, to study 2+1d anyon proliferation and to construct logical gates in non-Abelian topological quantum codes. The functions in AlgebrasTQD compute all condensable algebras $A(H,F,γ,ε)$ with their algebra structure and anyons, sort them into isomorphism classes and identify the twin algebras that are inequivalent but share the same anyon decomposition. AlgebrasTQD also determines all the subalgebra relations, the corresponding Hasse diagram and the types of 1+1d phases for any chosen, possibly non-invertible, group-theoretical symmetry. For non-maximal algebras, the code computes the reduced topological order with its $3$-cocycle and the map of anyons across the topological interface. Finally, AlgebrasTQD includes functions that construct certain non-Clifford logical gates via non-Abelian surface codes. We summarize the formulas that are implemented in the AlgebrasTQD code, and we illustrate its use with Abelian and non-Abelian groups including $\mathbb{Z}_2^3$ with $ω_\text{III}$, $D_8$, $A_4$ with and without anomaly, twin algebras and twin phases, and the Clifford-hierarchy gates in quantum doubles of dihedral groups. All arithmetic is exact and AlgebrasTQD can be used with any finite group $G$ and 3-cocycle $ω$, limited only by the time and size of the calculation. The code is available at https://github.com/AlisonWarman/AlgebrasTQD △ Less

These two classes of systems are closely related through the Holstein--Primakoff transformation (HPT). In this work, we show that the HPT offers novel insights into inherently quantum-mechanical detection of new physics. We begin by discussing how the HPT can be used to unify certain existing qubit- and boson-based proposals in a single framework. We demonstrate that these novel states can be readily prepared, and combine the coherent advantage of GHZ states with superior error rejection.     Authors: Ankit Sonthalia, Arnas Uselis, Seong Joon Oh

    Abstract: …same transformation regardless of whether it is applied to a kitchen or a carpet? In vision, the same concept can manifest in visually unrelated "regimes" that rarely co-occur: images of digits and hands… ▽ More Do vision models encode the transition $2{\to}3$ the same way whether it appears in handwritten digits or in hand gestures, and do they "understand" that clean${\to}$dirty is the same transformation regardless of whether it is applied to a kitchen or a carpet? The linear representation hypothesis (LRH) answers this for language models: concept changes correspond to directions in (language) embedding space. In vision, the same concept can manifest in visually unrelated "regimes" that rarely co-occur: images of digits and hands do not share much in common in pixel space. To understand whether vision models still encode the underlying abstract concepts, we study 46 vision encoders across architectures, training objectives, and scales, on 10 concept families including numerosity, age, object identity, and MIT-States attributes. Within each regime, we find statistically significant concept directions near-universally across our tested models. However, two distinct regimes do not always share the same concept direction. Several models learn multiple linear structures for the same concept transition, split across regimes. We call this phenomenon "fragmented linearity": strong local linearity coexisting with near-absent global linearity. Fragmentation is, however, not fixed: models with stronger local linearity progressively escape it (local-global linearity correlated with $τ\in [0.38, 0.89]$ across all concepts), and where global linearity does emerge, concept directions can transfer as linear probes across regimes. △ Less

    The Kubo-Thermalization Correspondence

    Authors: Songtao Huang, Xingyu Li, Jianyi Chen, Alan Tsidilkovski, Gabriel G. T. Assumpção, Pengfei Zhang, Hui Zhai, Nir Navon

    Abstract: …perspectives-long-time equilibration versus short-time response-seem fundamentally disconnected. Here we establish an exact link between them: the \emph{Kubo-Thermalization correspondence}, which connects long-time thermalized magnetization under weak driving to short-time linear-response spectra for a spin coupled to a thermal bath. The… ▽ More Quantum thermalization describes how interacting quantum systems relax toward thermal equilibrium, a central problem in modern physics. Yet most experimental information on many-body systems comes from short-time transition spectroscopy, typically interpreted within Kubo's linear-response framework. These perspectives-long-time equilibration versus short-time response-seem fundamentally disconnected. Here we establish an exact link between them: the \emph{Kubo-Thermalization correspondence}, which connects long-time thermalized magnetization under weak driving to short-time linear-response spectra for a spin coupled to a thermal bath. The correspondence holds even when the steady state differs substantially from the initial state and when each regime is individually difficult to describe theoretically. We experimentally confirm the correspondence using effective spin-$1/2$ impurities realized with ultracold fermions in two internal states coupled to a Fermi sea. Our results provide a rare exact statement about quantum thermalization and offer a novel route to infer thermalization dynamics from equilibrium response measurements in strongly interacting quantum systems, independent of microscopic details of the system--bath coupling. △ Less

We find that the capture of charges near the qubits can strongly disrupt the conditional operation, while the nanometer-scale motion of charges results in a loss of fidelity that sensitively depends on the position and strength of the corresponding dipole.     Information-theoretic entropy imaging of quantum matter

    Authors: Chen Wu, Leonard W. Cao, Lingyuan Lyu, Liam Cohen, Jiahui Nie, Can Uzundal, Yujia Hu, Quanjin Wang, Ziying Yan, Sneh Pancholi, Kenji Watanabe, Takashi Taniguchi, Feng Wang, Andrea F. Young, Tarun Grover, Monica T. Allen

    Abstract: Phase transitions can generate intricate spatial organization invisible to spatially averaged probes, yet quantitative measures of this organization remain limited. Here we develop "entropy imaging," an information-theoretic technique that harnesses lossless compression to quantify spatial complexity, predictability, and order in quantum materials. Using this approach, emergent spatial textures ca… ▽ More Phase transitions can generate intricate spatial organization invisible to spatially averaged probes, yet quantitative measures of this organization remain limited. Here we develop "entropy imaging," an information-theoretic technique that harnesses lossless compression to quantify spatial complexity, predictability, and order in quantum materials. Using this approach, emergent spatial textures can be imaged and converted into an experimentally-accessible observable: the computable information density (CID), a compression-based measure of information complexity that approaches the Shannon entropy rate in the large-system limit. Applied to millikelvin microwave impedance microscopy of graphene, the method reveals a pronounced maximum in the CID near quantum Hall transitions, a quantitative measure of the information-rich spatial organization of the critical landscape. A disordered Hofstadter model reproduces this behavior and shows that the CID can identify the quantum critical point from a sequence of images, without ensemble averaging over disorder realizations. The same framework tracks fractional quantum Hall transitions and resolves isolated fractional puddles that coexist with a disordered background. By contrast, the trivial band-edge crossover in a MoSe2/WS2 moiré heterobilayer exhibits qualitatively different behavior, suggesting that the pronounced CID enhancement observed at the quantum Hall critical point is not simply a generic feature of a conductive-insulating crossover. We further show that the geometric information encoded in CID is not reducible to a correlation length, and it can even distinguish spatial textures with identical multifractal statistics. Together, these results establish lossless compression as a framework for quantifying emergent spatial organization in quantum materials, with portability across imaging modalities and physical systems. △ Less

The proof uses the ramification of a degree-$r$ morphism to the projective line, Castelnuovo--Severi, and explicit automorphisms of the covering curve.     Authors: Péter L. Erdős, Gábor Lippner, Na'ama Nevo, Lajos Soukup

    Abstract: …-stability and rapid-mixing results for directed degree-sequence regions. The encoding process allows most corresponding statements for the two cases to be formulated and proved in one run. ▽ More The switch Markov chain is a natural method for sampling graph realizations with a prescribed degree sequence, and $P$-stability is a general sufficient condition for its rapid mixing. Since $P$-stability is often difficult to verify directly, we develop an approach based on the simpler and more readily verifiable property of \emph{full graphicality}. We consider bidegree sequence regions obtained by fixing the two class sizes, the common degree sum, and lower and upper bounds for the degrees in each class. Such a region is fully graphic if every bidegree sequence satisfying these constraints has a bipartite realization. Our main result shows that every fully graphic region of this form is $P$-stable. More precisely, for class sizes $n$ and $m$, the perturbation ratio is at most $(nm)^5$. It follows from a known theory that the switch Markov chain is rapidly mixing on every such region. We also show that full graphicality can be tested on a canonical extremal bidegree sequence and use this characterization to derive several new explicit sufficient conditions. By encoding loopless directed graphs as bipartite graphs, we develop a parallel theory for directed degree sequences. This gives analogous extremal characterizations, sufficient conditions for full graphicality, and $P$-stability and rapid-mixing results for directed degree-sequence regions. The encoding process allows most corresponding statements for the two cases to be formulated and proved in one run. △ Less

In the last section of the paper, assuming that the initial departure rate is not a Nash equilibrium, we consider an evolution equation for the departure rate, where each driver keeps changing his daily departure time in order to reduce his own cost. It is proved that this nonlocal integro-differential evolution equation admits a unique solution, globally in time. This provides some theoretical support to earlier numerical simulations, which indicated the dynamic instability of the Nash equilibrium and the possible existence of a chaotic attractor. △ Less

The metric $d_0$ coincides with the resistance metric on graphs with Euclidean edges, and thus extends it… ▽ More Isotropic Gaussian processes on networks have a covariance that is a function of a distance between locations, but valid constructions have so far required graphs with Euclidean edges and the resistance metric. We introduce a one-parameter family of metrics $d_κ$, $κ\ge0$, on arbitrary compact metric graphs, including those with loops and parallel edges, defined as the variograms of first-order Whittle--Matérn fields, where $κ=0$ corresponds to an intrinsic limiting field. The metric $d_0$ coincides with the resistance metric on graphs with Euclidean edges, and thus extends it to general compact metric graphs without any modification of the graph. For every completely monotone function $r$ and every $κ\ge0$, we show that $r(d_κ)$ is a positive semidefinite function. This yields isotropic Gaussian processes on general compact metric graphs. For $κ>0$ the metric levels off beyond the screening length $κ^{-1}$, which can be used to induce long-range dependence in short-range covariance models, and $κ$ can be estimated from data together with the parameters of $r$. The models are compared on daily traffic maps from the street network of Paris and the motorway network around London. For both, $κ$ is estimated to be positive, $d_κ$ increases the maximised log-likelihood relative to $d_0$ for every covariance function, and it gives the best predictions at randomly held-out locations. △ Less

Semiclassically, the OPE correlation acquires an oscillatory phase governed by hyperbolic volume, whose angle derivatives measure Lorentzian holonomies. As four necks of the future boundary pinch and the remaining bends vanish, its genus-three surface degenerates into two four-punctured spheres joined at four nodes. Sphere-tree sewing extends this relation between geometry and wavefunction coefficients to higher-point channels. △ Less

    Authors: Minjeong Song, Hyukjoon Kwon, Valerio Scarani

Third, we derive Lindbladian TTR co… ▽ More Channels $\mathcal{N}$ that describe open quantum dynamics are inherently irreversible: it is impossible to undo their effect completely, but one can study partial recovery of the information. The Petz recovery map $\hat{\mathcal{N}}_γ^{(\texttt{P})}$ is a systematic construction that depends only on $\mathcal{N}$ and on a reference state $γ$, which will be recovered exactly. If the real input state was different from $γ$, the recovery is partial, with a guarantee of near-optimality. Generically, an implementation of the Petz recovery map would look very different from the implementation of the channel. It is natural to study under which conditions the two maps require similar or even identical resources. The noisy forward channel $\mathcal{N}$ is called ``tabletop time-reversible'' for a given $γ$ when the corresponding Petz recovery map is realizable in such a way. First, we study the exact tabletop reversibility (TTR) conditions. We show in particular that a time-sensitive control of an ancilla system is needed. Second, we present the approximate TTR conditions, which do not require such a time-sensitive control. Third, we derive Lindbladian TTR conditions under a random-time collision model. △ Less

Despite this, most existing approximate unlearning methods tackle the problem from a post-hoc perspective. They attempt to erase the influence of targeted samples through parameter updates that typically require access to the full training data. This creates a mismatch with real deployment scenarios where unlearning requests can be anticipated, revealing a fundamental limitation of post-hoc approaches. We motivate unlearning by design, a novel paradigm for approximate methods in which models are directly trained to support forgetting as an inherent architectural capability. We instantiate this idea with Machine UNlearning via KEY deletion (MUNKEY), a memory-augmented transformer that decouples instance-specific memorization from model weights. Here, unlearning corresponds to removing the instance-identifying key, enabling zero-shot forgetting without weight updates or access to the original samples or labels. Our results establish that unlearning by design enables fast, deployment-oriented unlearning while preserving predictive performance. △ Less

J. Probab., 2020). As pointed out by Bassetti, Bourguin, Campese, and Peccati (Statist. Probab. Lett., 2026) this distance alone does not imply weak convergence in infinite dimensions, we also consider $d_2^\dagger$, obtained by adding the absolute difference of the second moments. Nevertheless, they primarily optimize these selection mechanisms independently along a single input-side dimension, overlooking their shared basic form and the inherent dependencies across input levels, thereby limiting the effectiveness of input-side dimensional configuration.     Verse: Semantics and Verification of Hybrid Multi-Agent Scenarios

    Authors: Yangge Li, Katherine Braught, Alex Yuan, Sayan Mitra

    Abstract: …in a familiar programming language syntax, with a formal semantics for the resulting scenarios. Reachability analysis on this automaton builds an execution tree from two post operators, one for continuous flow over a time step and one fo… ▽ More Verifying cyber-physical systems with many interacting agents remains difficult because interactions are mediated by sensors, individual decisions are constrained by environment (map) structure, and the resulting mode space scales combinatorially. We present Verse, a framework for writing agents, maps, and sensors in a familiar programming language syntax, with a formal semantics for the resulting scenarios. A compiler translates each scenario to a hybrid automaton whose executions correspond exactly to the runs of the scenario. Reachability analysis on this automaton builds an execution tree from two post operators, one for continuous flow over a time step and one for a single agent's mode change, and checks safety assertions at every vertex. The tree expands only the modes that are reached, without enumerating the global mode space. Experiments highlight expressive modeling across cars and drones, 2D/3D maps (including OpenDRIVE imports), noisy sensors, and multiple simultaneous safety assertions. Verse verifies a scenario with 7 agents and 37 modes in 90 seconds on a standard laptop; it is competitive with CORA and JuliaReach on ARCH benchmarks, and on multi-agent scenarios is an order of magnitude faster than JuliaReach at the cost of looser reachable sets. △ Less

    Authors: Jani A. Virtanen

    Abstract: …, no corresponding uniform estimate holds, even for smooth compactly supported symbols. We also determine the optimal constants in the Berger--Coburn estimates for $1/4<t\le1$. We prove that the reverse implication also fails, even for a real-valued admissible symbol $g$ with $g^{(1/4)}\in C_0(\mathbb{C}^n)$, and that the forward implication fails even when $T_g\in S_p$ for every finite $p>1$. Our counterexamples come from Baire category and closed graph arguments, which yield no explicit symbols. Despite this two-sided failure, the forward implication has a sharp Schatten-class form: $\|g^{(1/4)}\|_\infty\le 2^n\|T_g\|_{S_1}$ for every admissible symbol $g$ with $T_g$ trace class. We show that the constant $2^n$ is optimal and that the same estimate holds with $S_p$ in place of $S_1$ for $T_g\in S_p$ when $0<p\le1$. For $p>1$, no corresponding uniform estimate holds, even for smooth compactly supported symbols. We also determine the optimal constants in the Berger--Coburn estimates for $1/4<t\le1$. Finally, for $1\le p<\infty$, we show that the equivalence $T_g\in S_p$ if and only if $g^{(1/4)}\in L^p$ holds exactly when $p=2$, answering a question of Bauer, Coburn, and Isralowitz. △ Less

Intermittent structures, such as current sheets, are identified using the Partial Variance of Increments (PVI) method, and the scale-dependent normalized magnetic helicity is computed over scales corresponding to 0.5-3 correlation lengths. Prior studies have typically applied the PVI-helicity method to individual events; here we extend this technique to the ensemble statistics of a multi-month sample spanning many correlation times. To separate coherent helical structures from random turbulent fluctuations, significance thresholds for helicity are determined using phase-randomized surrogate realizations. We find a clear scale dependence in the occurrence, duration, and waiting time of coherent magnetic helicity, reminiscent of the scale-transfer arguments underlying inverse cascade, selective decay, and Taylor relaxation theories. Event duration and waiting-time distributions are well described by power-law and exponential models over different ranges, consistent with previous findings.     Evolve on Graph: Self-Evolving Knowledge Graph Reasoning without Question Annotations

    Authors: Zongyuan Sun, Shuai Zhao, Ke Shu, Ziyue Zhu, Shangyang Wu, Yuanlin Lin, Tao Wang, Shiyue Li, Yingjun Du, Anh Tuan Luu, Haoran Luo

    Abstract: …and a Solver that answers them. To make questions verifiable, Symbolically Verifiable Topology Synthesis (SVTS) samples reasoning topologies on the KG and executes the corresponding first-order logic queries, yielding topology skeletons with correct and complete answer sets; guided by a skeleton, the Proposer explores the graph to pose a multi-hop question.… ▽ More Knowledge graphs (KGs) provide reliable external knowledge for multi-hop factual reasoning with large language models (LLMs), yet most KGs in real-world domains such as finance and industry lack question--answer (QA) annotations. Without such annotations, existing methods struggle to improve LLM graph reasoning: training-free agents cannot improve their exploration through interaction; reinforcement learning (RL) agents depend on QA data annotated by humans or synthesized by closed-source models; and training data is typically built offline, so its topology mix is fixed and cannot follow the agent's ability. We propose Evolve on Graph (EoG), a framework that lets an LLM self-evolve its graph reasoning without any QA annotations. Through Graph Self-Play, a single LLM alternates between a Proposer that poses questions and a Solver that answers them. To make questions verifiable, Symbolically Verifiable Topology Synthesis (SVTS) samples reasoning topologies on the KG and executes the corresponding first-order logic queries, yielding topology skeletons with correct and complete answer sets; guided by a skeleton, the Proposer explores the graph to pose a multi-hop question. To let task topologies follow the Solver, a Boundary-Aware Graph Curriculum rewards the Proposer by the Solver's score variance, keeping questions at the Solver's capability boundary so that complex topologies enter training as the Solver improves. On six benchmarks over Freebase, Wikidata, and an industrial KG, EoG consistently outperforms prompting-, retrieval-, and agent-based methods, showing that LLM graph reasoning can improve through self-play without QA annotations. Our code is available at https://github.com/Szy5/EoG △ Less

    Nonclassicality of a Macroscopic Qubit-Ensemble via Parity Measurement Induced Disturbance

    Authors: Lorenzo Braccini, Debarshi Das, Ben Zindorf, Stephen D. Hogan, John J. L. Morton, Sougato Bose

    Abstract: …environmental decoherence and inhomogeneity of qubit-electromagnetic field couplings precipitate the quantum-to-classical transition. This illustrates that Bohr's correspondence principle is not fundamental, but a consequence of practical limitations. We outline an implementation with a variety of qubits (superconducting qubits, spins in semiconductors,… ▽ More We propose an experimental scheme to test the nonclassicality of a macroscopic ensemble of qubits, through the violation of the classical notion of macrorealism (MR) via the fundamental measurement-induced disturbance of quantum systems. An electromagnetic resonator is used to probe the parity of the qubit-ensemble. The action of sequential measurements allows the nonclassicality of whole ensemble to manifest itself, in the ideal case, irrespective of its size. This enables to probe the macroscopic limits of quantum mechanics as the qubit-ensemble is, effectively, a single large spin of many $\hbar$ units. Even as $\hbar \rightarrow 0$ in comparison to the total angular momentum of the ensemble, a constant amount of violation of MR is found in the noiseless case. However, environmental decoherence and inhomogeneity of qubit-electromagnetic field couplings precipitate the quantum-to-classical transition. This illustrates that Bohr's correspondence principle is not fundamental, but a consequence of practical limitations. We outline an implementation with a variety of qubits (superconducting qubits, spins in semiconductors, and Rydberg atoms) coupled to a coplanar waveguide resonator, and -- via the corresponding noise analysis -- find that violation of MR is detectable up to $100$ qubits via current technology. △ Less

    hep-th math-ph nlin.PS nlin.SI

    Variable-mass sine-Gordon with point defects: integrability, soliton transmission, and quasi-conservation

    Authors: A. R. Aguirre, H. Blas, H. F. Callisaya, M. C. de Oliveira

    Abstract: …variable-mass sine-Gordon model (vmSG) with point defects. Using the Lax and Bäcklund-gauge formulations, we construct type-I and type-II defect matrices, derive the corresponding sewing conditions, and generate the bulk and defect contributions to an infinite hierarchy of conserved charges. The lowest members reduce to the standard sine-Gordon energy and mo… ▽ More We study integrable variable-mass sine-Gordon model (vmSG) with point defects. Using the Lax and Bäcklund-gauge formulations, we construct type-I and type-II defect matrices, derive the corresponding sewing conditions, and generate the bulk and defect contributions to an infinite hierarchy of conserved charges. The lowest members reduce to the standard sine-Gordon energy and momentum in the homogeneous limit, while for inhomogeneous backgrounds they define integrability-generated energy- and momentum-type quantities. Defect compatibility imposes matching conditions on the variable-mass functions across the defect. An analytical transmission factor $z$ characterizes soliton transmission, topological conversion, and absorption/emission processes. We then deform the type-I sewing conditions by parameters $α$ and $β$ and derive the associated defect anomalies. Consistent one-soliton transmission is recovered for $αβ=1$, whereas for $αβ\neq 1$ parity-centered kink-kink and kink-antikink transmissions display vanishing lowest order integrated anomalies but no generic vanishing at higher order. Numerical simulations reproduce the integrable transmission/conversion regimes and show that non-integrable defects generate small residual disturbances and lowest order quasi-conserved charges, while quasi-conservation of the higher order charges remains unestablished. These results elucidate how exact defect integrability deforms into charge-dependent quasi-conservation and establish a framework for defect-controlled soliton transport in inhomogeneous media, with potential applications to nonuniform Josephson junctions, magnetic and nonlinear-optical systems, and effective molecular and DNA models. △ Less

    Web of Non-invertible Dualities for (2+1) Dimensional Models with Subsystem Symmetries

    Authors: Avijit Maity, Vikram Tripathi, Andriy H. Nevidomskyy

    Abstract: …strong SSPT order: although strictly local SSPT repair operators map to highly nonlocal objects in the dual SSSB phase, the essential commutation algebra and the bulk-edge correspondence remain intact. ▽ More We extend non-invertible duality concepts from one-dimensional systems to two spatial dimensions by constructing a web of non-invertible dualities for lattice models with subsystem symmetries. For the $\mathbb{Z}_2 \times \mathbb{Z}_2$ subsystem symmetry on the square lattice, we build two complementary dualities: a map that sends spontaneous subsystem symmetry-broken (SSSB) phases to the trivial phase (the analogue of the Kramers-Wannier (KW) duality in 1+1D), and a generalized subsystem Kennedy-Tasaki (KT) transformation that maps SSSB phases to subsystem symmetry-protected topological (SSPT) phases while leaving the trivial phase invariant. These dualities are boundary-sensitive. On open lattices, both subsystem KW and KT transformations act as unitary, invertible operators. In particular, the KT map not only matches the bulk Hamiltonians of the dual phases but also carries the spontaneous ground-state degeneracy of the SSSB phase onto the protected boundary degeneracy of the SSPT phase. On closed manifolds, however, both maps become intrinsically non-unitary and non-invertible when restricted to the original Hilbert space. We demonstrate this non-invertibility via ground-state degeneracy matching (in two copies of the Xu-Moore/Ising-plaquette model), analysis of symmetry-twist sectors mapping, and the fusion algebra of the duality operator. Enlarging the Hilbert space to include twisted sectors allows the subsystem KW map to be formulated as a projective unitary preserving quantum transition probabilities, consistent with generalized Wigner-theorem-based constructions. We also show that the KT map faithfully transmits the algebraic content of bulk and edge invariants diagnosing strong SSPT order: although strictly local SSPT repair operators map to highly nonlocal objects in the dual SSSB phase, the essential commutation algebra and the bulk-edge correspondence remain intact. △ Less

We then refine the candidate pose through optimization, enforcing consistency between the optimized hand pose and the generated Distance Profile in near-cont… ▽ More Generating 3D hand-object interactions is essential for applications in robotics, AR/VR, and synthetic data generation, where flexible controllability and strong generalization to diverse object geometries are required. However, existing methods rarely satisfy these requirements, limiting their practical applicability. We present DCGrasp, a multi-modal diffusion framework for distance-aware controllable grasp generation. DCGrasp is built on a novel grasp energy term that computes Distance Profile, a signed distance from each hand vertex to the nearest object point, coupled with distance-aware weighting, effectively capturing the semantically similar hand-object interaction in near-contact regions while remaining invariant to object and hand identity. Given various controllable signals, DCGrasp first generates a Distance Profile based on a Diffusion Transformer, together with a corresponding candidate hand pose. We then refine the candidate pose through optimization, enforcing consistency between the optimized hand pose and the generated Distance Profile in near-contact regions. Our experiments show that DCGrasp produces high-quality, geometrically plausible grasps with flexible user control, generalizing to diverse object and hand shapes and scales. Our work establishes a robust and versatile pipeline for the synthesis of controllable 3D hand-object interactions. The supplementary video with dynamic visualization is available on our project page. △ Less

The weak modular property of these modular forms allows us to use representation theo… ▽ More We study the equidistribution of integers of the form $n= x_1^2 + \cdots + x_d^2$ under the arithmetic constraints given by $(\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z})^d$. Finally, we conclude that the equidistribution phenomenon occurs locally on $p+1$ orbits for $d \geq 4$. △ Less
