    cs.CL

    Circuit Distillation

    Authors: Somin Wadhwa, Silvio Amir, Byron C. Wallace

    Abstract: …introduces an objective to align internal representations between analogous circuit components in teacher and student models. We propose a method to match ``functionally correspondent'' circuit components and introduce a loss reflecting similarities between the representations that these induce. We evaluate circuit distillation on entity tracking and… ▽ More Model distillation typically focuses on behavioral mimicry, where a student model is trained to replicate a teacher's output while treating its internal computations as a black box. In this work we propose an alternative approach: Distilling the underlying computational mechanisms implemented by a teacher model. Specifically, we propose circuit distillation, which introduces an objective to align internal representations between analogous circuit components in teacher and student models. We propose a method to match ``functionally correspondent'' circuit components and introduce a loss reflecting similarities between the representations that these induce. We evaluate circuit distillation on entity tracking and theory of mind (ToM) tasks using models from the Llama3 family. Our results demonstrate that circuit distillation outperforms standard distillation, successfully transferring algorithmic capabilities by adjusting only a small, targeted subset of student model parameters. This work establishes the feasibility of transferring mechanisms, which may in turn allow for efficient distillation of targeted teacher capabilities via interpretable and controllable internal student mechanisms. △ Less

    Minimum Time Consensus of Multi-agent System under Fuel Constraints

    Authors: Akansha Rautela, Deepak Patil, Ameer Mulla, Indra Narayan Kar

    Abstract: …the set of initial conditions from which consensus is possible. The minimum time to consensus is the least time at which the attainable sets of all agents intersect, and the corresponding consensus state is their point of intersection. Using convexity of the attainable set and Helly's theorem, this reduces to examining the triplets of agents, for which a… ▽ More This work addresses the problem of finding the minimum time consensus point in the state space for a set of $N$ identical double integrator agents with bounded input and fixed fuel budget constraints. The attainable set of each agent under these constraints is computed, and its boundary is used to characterize the set of initial conditions from which consensus is possible. The minimum time to consensus is the least time at which the attainable sets of all agents intersect, and the corresponding consensus state is their point of intersection. Using convexity of the attainable set and Helly's theorem, this reduces to examining the triplets of agents, for which a closed-form expression for the minimum time and the consensus point is obtained. The triplet computations are independent and are distributed evenly among the $N$ agents. The overall minimum time and consensus point are given by the triplet with the greatest minimum time, and each agent then computes its own control input locally. An example with $12$ agents is used to illustrate the results. △ Less

Let $X \subset \mathbb{R}^d$ be a set of $n$ points, labeled by a collection of $k$ boxes: the label of each point in $X$ is determined by the unique box containing it. A learner aims to exactly recover the labels for every point in $X$ given access to a label oracle. We consider three increasingly-structured settings. In the most general setting, there are no conditions on the boxes, and they may overlap. We show that CAL (Cohn-Atlas-Ladner 1994), a classical active learning algorithm, is minimax-optimal with query complexity $Θ(kd \log (n/kd))$. When the boxes are disjoint, we examine two regimes. In the large-$k$ regime, CAL remains minimax-optimal. For the constant-$k$ regime, an optimized variant of the Halving algorithm (Hegedüs 1995) can beat CAL by a $\log(d)$ factor. Finally, in the most-structured settings, the boxes correspond to the leaves of a constant-sized decision tree. We prove a tight query complexity of $Θ(d + \log n)$ that is additive in $d$ and $\log n$, generalizing a recent result (Black-Larsen-Mazumdar-Saha-So 2026) that establishes the query complexity of learning decision stumps. △ Less

    Benchmarking quantum-annealing-based approaches for factory layout planning

    Authors: Xiangqian Wu, Philipp Schworm, Marcel Wagner, Jan C. Aurich

    Abstract: …types that combine QA and classical methods. To explore the potential of QA for factory layout planning, this paper benchmarks four QA based approaches implemented with the corresponding solvers, including an iterative binary quadratic model (IBQM), a hybrid discrete quadratic model (HDQM), a hybrid constrained quadratic model (HCQM), and a hybrid nonlinear… ▽ More The earlystage of factory layout planning has a direct effect on the operating costs, efficiency, and productivity of manufacturing systems. Conventional automated planning approaches often face a trade-off between solution quality and computational effort. Recent studies have investigated the potential of quantum annealing (QA) for solving factory layout planning problems. However, existing studies have mainly focused on individual QA-based formulations, whereas current hybrid QA solvers support multiple model types that combine QA and classical methods. To explore the potential of QA for factory layout planning, this paper benchmarks four QA based approaches implemented with the corresponding solvers, including an iterative binary quadratic model (IBQM), a hybrid discrete quadratic model (HDQM), a hybrid constrained quadratic model (HCQM), and a hybrid nonlinear model (HNLM). To improve scalability, an iterative decomposition strategy is developed for IBQM, while a candidate selection procedure is introduced for HDQM. The approaches are benchmarked against genetic algorithm, particle swarm optimization, and a hybrid tabu search and simulated annealing approach on 14 layout instances with different layout sizes, geometric complexity, spatial restrictions, and transport route configurations. The comparative analysis considers solution quality, computation time, and scalability. The results show that QA-based approaches achieve competitive solution quality and substantial computational advantages, reducing computation time by up to 94.51% relative to the classical reference methods. Among them, IBQM obtains the best solution quality in 78.6% of the instances, with a maximum improvement of 17.41% associated with an 81.34% reduction in computation time. These findings provide a basis for their further application in industrial layout planning. △ Less

Leveraging the Extreme Learning Machine (ELM) framework, the proposed XL MIMO system architecture is shown to be a universal approximator, and the optimal linear-SIM-based combining is derived in closed form, allowing for fast training even and partial signal observability. These capacities form a normalized, nondecreasing submodular rank function whose base polytope is exactly the thermally reachable population region. Their bounds therefore provide a complete system of accessibility constraints. Every nested chain of capacities can be simultaneously saturated by a reachable state, yielding the maximum expectation value of any prescribed observable, including the system Hamiltonian. For a prescribed target, its thermomajorization prefixes suffice to certify exact conversion through at most $n-1$ binary work-extraction probes. Finally, the optimal trace-distance error equals the largest capacity advantage of the target over the initial state. This gap is attained at a target prefix and equals the largest positive optimized energy-storage deficit among the same binary probes, divided by their energy gap. Thus a common population capacity geometry determines accessibility, observable optimization, conversion certificates, and universal approximation. △ Less

    Theory and Cosmology of Biverse Volume-Normalized Gravity

    Authors: Siqing Yu

    Abstract: …the massive gravity theory has a second metric tensor with questionable origin and the quantum strong coupling problem. In the literature, there exist two interesting ideas: 1) to use the "volume-normalized" action functional, the usual action of the particle Standard Model and general relativity but divided by the spacetime volume, first proposed by Tseytlin; 2) to generate the vacuum energy density dynamically by assigning a ghost-free massive potential to the graviton. However, each has a problem: Tseytlin's attempt suffers from diverging quantum loops and the massive gravity theory has a second metric tensor with questionable origin and the quantum strong coupling problem. Inspired by these two ideas, as well as the AdS/CFT correspondence in string theory, the biverse volume-normalized gravity extends Tseytlin's idea by placing our (3+1)-dimensional universe on the boundary of a (4+1)-dimensional bulk space. This new adaptation controls the large quantum gravity loops in the limit of vanishing ratio of the spacetime volume of our universe to that of the other universe. The ghost-free massive gravity can then be seamlessly incorporated, not only that its otherwise nebulous second metric tensor appears naturally as the pullback of the bulk metric on the boundary, but its strong coupling problem also solved. The biverse volume-normalized gravity is also consistent with all tested models of particle cosmology and the evolution of the universe, and nontrivially predicts that the tensor-to-scalar ratio of our universe is exactly zero. △ Less

In nonzero curvature such a factorization exists locally exactly when the principal curvatures are locally constant; the normalized Euclidean branch is equally rigid, and the remaining real spherical non-CMC branch is excluded by a finite-type Frobenius obstruction. For $m>2$, this scalar rigidity does not imply geometric nonexistence. The conformal tension has independent normal and tangential components. We prove that $\mathbb Rξ\oplus TM$ is the minimal natural $O(m)$-equivariant carrier, derive the corresponding strongly elliptic Jacobi block and its two-grade Clifford realization, and identify off-diagonal hybridization as the escape mechanism. Exact cohomogeneity-one reductions, combined with validated interval and Chebyshev/radii-polynomial arguments, yield embedded proper non-CMC, nowhere-umbilical examples in round spheres, including $$ S^1\times\mathbb S^n\times\mathbb S^n\hookrightarrow\mathbb S^{2n+2} \qquad(n\ge1). $$ Thus the scalar obstruction is sharp with respect to coefficient bundle: the smallest enlargement forced by conformal tension already supports compact non-CMC solutions. △ Less

    Unique direct-sum decompositions of infinite-dimensional unitary representations of the infinite dihedral group via real CMV matrices

    Abstract: …is unique up to unitary equivalence when the summands arising from real CMV matrices are grouped according to their multiplicities and the spectral measures of the corresponding matrices are mutually singular across distinct multiplicities. Furthermore, we show that the representation associated with each such real CMV matrix $C$ admits a direct integral dec… ▽ More Unitary representations of the infinite dihedral group arise naturally and play an important role in a wide range of areas including mathematics, physics, and engineering. CMV matrices, introduced by Cantero, Moral, and Velázquez, are five-diagonal unitary matrices that are standard tools in the theory of orthogonal polynomials on the unit circle. It is known that every unitary representation of the infinite dihedral group on a separable Hilbert space admits a discrete direct-sum decomposition into representations arising from the natural factorizations of real CMV matrices and copies of two exceptional one-dimensional irreducible representations. We prove that such a decomposition is unique up to unitary equivalence when the summands arising from real CMV matrices are grouped according to their multiplicities and the spectral measures of the corresponding matrices are mutually singular across distinct multiplicities. Furthermore, we show that the representation associated with each such real CMV matrix $C$ admits a direct integral decomposition into irreducible unitary representations with respect to the push-forward of the restriction of the spectral measure of $C$ to the closed upper half circle. These results make it possible to study the structure of unitary representations of the infinite dihedral group using CMV matrices and the theory of orthogonal polynomials on the unit circle. △ Less

We built an open-source reconstruction, gdelt-regkg, and measured how closely it agrees with GDELT's archived (native) records for 193,550 English articles from 8,729 publishers (2020-2025) and 2,500 articles in five other languages. Agreement depends strongly on the field. English tone, the percentage of positive minus negative words, has a mean absolute error (MAE) of 1.79 points and a concordance correlation coefficient of 0.75. It is lower for date components (0.42), complete counts with their objects and locations (0.07), and quotations (0.07), while GCAM category presence reaches micro F1 0.84 within the implemented dictionaries. In subsets where retrieved and native titles and lengths match, tone error falls to 1.47 and organization F1 rises to 0.48, yet most disagreement remains. A larger gazetteer raises coverage of native places from 89.5% to 97.1% with little change in location F1. Tone computed on English translations of non-English articles is less accurate (MAE 2.10-2.64) than for English articles. The results are conditional on article pages that could still be retrieved, and the correspondence subsets do not isolate the effect of input differences. Reconstructed records should therefore be assessed field by field and not treated as completely interchangeable with native ones. △ Less

Its impact has penetrated into all aspects of algorithm design, especially in the field of cryptography. The security of cryptographic algorithms based on short keys depends on whether P is equal to NP. In fact, the security requirements for cryptographic keys are much stricter than those for P$\neq$NP, the security of the key must ensure not only a sufficiently high computational complexity to crack it, but also consider the security of each bit of the key, while fully avoiding the effectiveness of various attack methods. In this paper, we innovatively propose a new encoding mechanism and develop a novel block symmetric encryption algorithm, which be named Eagle, whose encryption and decryption can be completed in linear time. The key consists of 10 variables, for the attacker, in the case when only the plaintext-ciphertext correspondence is known, the problem of cracking the key is equivalent to solving a system of equations about ten unknown variables together with some implicit variables which are generated by the encryption process. In this paper, we will prove that there is no universal algorithm without brute-force searching at least one variable during the process of verifying four of the ten variables whose computational complexity is not greater than solving the ten variables. After performing elimination the implicit variables, the problem of verifying the four variables will be equivalently transformed into a series of general quintic equations about one variable with coefficient given by other four variables, according to the conclusion that ``there is no algebraic solution to quintic equations", we can't establish a system of algebraic equations only containing the other four variables...... △ Less

    Authors: The MMGPDs Collaboration, Muhammad Goharipour, Anoushiravan Moradi, K. Azizi

    Abstract: …framework, we investigate the consistency among the different datasets, determine the proton electromagnetic FFs within several fitting scenarios, and extract the corresponding charge and magnetic radii. Using the same phenomenological framework, we investigate the consistency among the different datasets, determine the proton electromagnetic FFs within several fitting scenarios, and extract the corresponding charge and magnetic radii. A significant tension is found between the CLAS 2018 measurements and the remaining EP data, with the low-$|t|$ region playing a dominant role in this tension. We systematically investigate this behavior through $|t|$-cut scans and assess its impact on the extracted FFs and radii. For all analyses, the extracted proton charge radius is smaller than the Particle Data Group average and most determinations based on elastic electron--proton scattering. However, the results are consistent, within uncertainties, with the PRad measurement and muonic hydrogen spectroscopy. In contrast, the magnetic radius is found to be compatible with the current world average. These results demonstrate that BH-dominated EP measurements provide an independent and complementary approach to determine the electromagnetic structure of the proton and offer additional support for the small-radius solution of the proton charge-radius puzzle. △ Less

    The monotone part of Chatterjee's rank correlation

    Authors: Karl Friedrich Siburg

    Abstract: …is, up to a factor, the squared distance to the polar cone, which corresponds to negative quadrant dependence. The monotone part vanishes exactly for negatively quadrant dependent copulas, equals $ξ$ exactly for stochastically increasing copulas, and equals one only for the comonotonicity copula. In contrast to $ξ$, it is increasing in the concordance order… ▽ More Chatterjee's rank correlation $ξ$ measures how strongly a random variable $Y$ depends functionally on a random variable $X$, but it does not distinguish increasing, decreasing and non-monotone relationships. We ask how much of the dependence measured by $ξ$ is explained by an increasing relationship. For continuous marginals, $ξ$ is, up to a factor, a squared distance from independence in a Hilbert space geometry derived from the Sobolev scalar product for copulas. We answer it by the metric projection onto the closed convex set of stochastically increasing copulas in this geometry. The projection is explicit: it replaces every vertical section by its least concave majorant, and it coincides with the smallest stochastically increasing copula dominating the given one. It splits $ξ$ orthogonally into a monotone part $ξ^{\uparrow}$ and the squared distance to the class of stochastically increasing copulas; dually, $ξ^{\uparrow}$ is, up to a factor, the squared distance to the polar cone, which corresponds to negative quadrant dependence. The monotone part vanishes exactly for negatively quadrant dependent copulas, equals $ξ$ exactly for stochastically increasing copulas, and equals one only for the comonotonicity copula. In contrast to $ξ$, it is increasing in the concordance order and continuous with respect to weak convergence. Symmetrisation decomposes the measure of mutual complete dependence in the same way. Finally, the construction extends to arbitrary distributions via isotonic regression of conditional distribution functions. In particular, the plug-in estimator based on the empirical distribution is strongly consistent for every copula, without smoothing or tuning parameters. △ Less

Finally, we formulate a variational problem over rational $Γ$-inner interpolants satisfying the prescribed two-point conditions. We show that the supremum of the associated outer-factor quantities determines the maximal admissible modulus of the first coordinate among pentablock-valued interpolants whose last two components belong to this class. Equivalently, this extremal problem reduces to minimizing the corresponding Green potential.     Authors: Samuel G. G. Johnston, Rohan Shiatis

We introduce a partition function on a finite version of this model and study the probabilistic properties of random pure snake configurations c… ▽ More A pure snake configuration is a bijection $σ:\mathbb{Z}^2 \to \mathbb{Z}^2$ containing no two-cycles and such that for each $x \in \mathbb{Z}^2$ we have $σ(x) \in \{ x , x+ \mathbf{e}^1, x+\mathbf{e}^2 , x- \mathbf{e}^2 \}.$ The non-trivial cycles of a pure snake configuration may be regarded as a collection of non-intersecting paths in $\mathbb{Z}^2$ that may travel right, up, or down (but not left) from a given vertex. Pure snake configurations are a generalisation of lozenge tilings, which are in natural correspondence with paths that only travel right or up. We introduce a partition function on a finite version of this model and study the probabilistic properties of random pure snake configurations chosen according to their contribution to this partition function. Under a suitable weighting, the model is integrable in the sense that we have access to explicit formulas for its partition function and correlation function. We utilise the integrable structure of this model in several applications through its various scaling limits, such as to prove a traffic representation of ASEP on the ring, generalising the analogous result for TASEP by the first author. △ Less

We introduce MechAlign, a framework for projec… ▽ More Lean verifies that a formal proof establishes its stated proposition under declared assumptions, but does not check whether that proposition faithfully expresses the mathematical claim in a manuscript. Assessing this correspondence requires interpreting the main theorem through its project dependencies, which may be scattered throughout a large development. We introduce MechAlign, a framework for project-level semantic alignment review that combines formal dependency extraction with LLM-based assessment. Its extraction tool, LeanTrace, constructs a statement dependency graph from elaborated declarations, retaining definition bodies and structure constraints while excluding proof bodies and stopping at an explicit library boundary. The reviewer uses this context to investigate alignment and report discrepancies with localized evidence. We conduct a preliminary review of 201 main results from 173 OpenAI Math projects, using supplied informal descriptions and available paper context. A hyperbolic-group case illustrates how dependency inspection localizes a missing dimensional constraint relative to the manuscript, consistent with the upstream declaration of formalization scope. We also construct LeanReviewBench, comprising 501 fixed claim-statement-context triples from OpenAI Math, Fermat's Last Theorem, and Kakeya, with description provenance recorded. The resource supports controlled comparisons of more project-level semantic alignment evaluations. △ Less

    Authors: Xiaofan Zhou, Lu Cheng

    Abstract: …first answer training questions, and groups of their saved responses guide parallel reflection and revision. After construction, an exploration policy selects candidates fr… ▽ More Reflective prompt optimization improves instructions using task feedback, but coupling candidate generation with full-validation-based selection can make search costly and sequential. We propose a two-stage framework that decouples reflective candidate-pool construction from validation allocation. During construction, sampled parents first answer training questions, and groups of their saved responses guide parallel reflection and revision. Each child is screened on its group's questions against the corresponding parent scores. Improving, nonduplicate prompts enter the pool and can become parents without full validation. After construction, an exploration policy selects candidates from the frozen pool under a fixed full-validation budget, and the prompt with the highest measured validation score is returned. This separation allows candidate-pool size and validation budget to be set independently. We evaluate random selection and a multilayer perceptron with similarity-based exploration (MLP + similarity) on five benchmarks using Qwen3.5-9B and Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct. With 100 retained candidates and 20 full validations, our framework improves over the initial solver by up to 11.22 percentage points. MLP + similarity improves over the initial solver in all ten comparisons, although neither selection policy consistently outperforms the other. These results support separating reflective search from budgeted validation while leaving room for stronger candidate-selection policies. △ Less

    Authors: Weijun Yin, Yanqing Yao, Falko Dressler, Di Wang, Youming Tao

    Abstract: …deletion, allowing exact unlearning to reuse computation through coupling. However, existing methods lack efficient mechanisms for exact unlearning to fully remove the influence of training data when users request deletion. A key insight is that total variation stability of the learning algorithm creates overlap between model distributions before and after data deletion, allowing exact unlearning to reuse computation through coupling. Based on this observation, we jointly design two stable zeroth-order learning algorithms and their corresponding exact-unlearning procedures: \textsc{FedZOO-S} obtains stability through client and mini-batch subsampling, enabling \textsc{FedZOUL-S} to perform efficient unlearning by preserving earlier updates and restarting from the first affected step. \textsc{FedZOO-NS} further combines subsampling with Gaussian noise to increase overlap between update distributions; \textsc{FedZOUL-NS} exploits this overlap through rejection--reflection coupling to reuse affected updates when the coupling is accepted. We prove exact unlearning for both algorithms: the model and saved state have the same joint distribution as retraining with the corresponding learner on retained data. We further establish stability and convergence guarantees for the learning algorithms and derive upper bounds on expected unlearning cost. Experiments demonstrate effective unlearning with competitive model utility and up to $56.1\%$ less cumulative unlearning time than re-optimization. Compared with first-order baselines, our methods reduce communication overhead by up to approximately four orders of magnitude. △ Less

    Authors: Sheila Zingg, Florian Tramèr, Martin Vechev, Srdjan Čapkun

    Abstract: …trustworthiness by reviewing the relevant literature. Thus, in order to trust a model, users still need to trust that developers acted honestly, reducing the benef… ▽ More There are different levels of transparency for LLMs: open weights, open data, and/or open pipeline. Recently, more interest has emerged in fully transparent LLMs, i.e., LLMs with fully open weights, data, and training pipeline. Drawing analogies to other fields such as software security, it is often believed that increasing transparency inherently increases the security and trustworthiness of the LLM. However, we argue that such claims need to be critically re-evaluated in the context of LLMs. In this paper, we analyze the impact of transparency on LLM security and trustworthiness by reviewing the relevant literature. Our core findings are that, due to the limited reproducibility of LLM pipelines, in practice, users cannot verify the correspondence between open weights and the associated open data and pipeline. Thus, in order to trust a model, users still need to trust that developers acted honestly, reducing the benefits over models that provide less "transparency". Moreover, we find that even when LLMs are fully transparent and trusted, this openness can favor adversaries. Open weights in particular, but also open data and pipelines, can increase the success rate of attacks. Although the same mechanisms can be used to improve the model and protect it against attacks, the current lack of robust lightweight mechanisms to patch LLM vulnerabilities limits the benefits of openness at this time. We note that our analysis is based on current LLM capabilities and analysis tools, and that future developments may require a re-evaluation of these points. △ Less

    cond-mat.quant-gas

    Phonon number relaxation in a 3D superfluid with a concave acoustic branch

    Authors: Yvan Castin, Mariia Tsimokha

    Abstract: …collisional amplitude with quantum hydrodynamics at low temperature, we determine the corresponding evolution of the fugacity $z_φ$ of the phonon gas from the non-degenerate regime $z_φ=0^+$ to complete equilibrium $z_φ=1^-$. Using the conservation of total energy, we find that the fugacity varies with a non-integer power law $\propto t^{4/5}$ at short time… ▽ More We consider the collisional evolution towards equilibrium of a spatially homogeneous and isotropic phonon gas of a three-dimensional superfluid with a concave acoustic excitation branch, at a non-zero but arbitrarily low temperature $T$. Three-phonon collisions $1φ\leftrightarrow 2φ$ are forbidden by conservation of energy-momentum. Four-phonon collisions $2φ\to 2φ$ of Landau and Khalatnikov lead, after a time $\propto T^{-7}$, only to a partial thermal equilibrium, a Bose law of non-zero chemical potential for the phonons, because they conserve the total number of phonons. Relaxation towards complete thermochemical equilibrium is therefore ensured by the much slower five-phonon collisions $2φ\leftrightarrow 3φ$ of Khalatnikov, in a time $\propto T^{-9}$. Using kinetic equations on the occupation numbers of the phonon modes and explicitly calculating the $2φ\to 3φ$ collisional amplitude with quantum hydrodynamics at low temperature, we determine the corresponding evolution of the fugacity $z_φ$ of the phonon gas from the non-degenerate regime $z_φ=0^+$ to complete equilibrium $z_φ=1^-$. Using the conservation of total energy, we find that the fugacity varies with a non-integer power law $\propto t^{4/5}$ at short times and an exponential law at long times; the speed of change of entropy, always positive, is asymptotically proportional to the square of the speed of change of fugacity, $(\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}t)S_φ\propto[(\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}t)z_φ]^2$, as Landau predicted for an arbitrarily slow adiabatic transformation. Our results bring to a close the study initiated by Khalatnikov in 1950 and could be experimentally verified in a gas of cold spin-1/2 fermionic atoms in the sector of the BEC-BCS crossover where the acoustic branch has a concave start, or in superfluid liquid helium-4 at sufficiently high pressure. △ Less

    Authors: Takeru Miyagawa, Willis O'Leary, Manuel Grumet, Hyunwon Chu, Jennifer L. M. Rupp, Waldemar Kaiser, David A. Egger

    Abstract: …lithium sublattice and gives rise to distinct features in their Raman spectra. A symmetry-resolved analysis further reveals that experimentally observed Raman peaks do not correspond to individual normal modes, but instead arise from overlapping contributions of multiple symmetry channels, challenging conventional peak-assignment approaches. By explicitly co… ▽ More Lithium lanthanum zirconate (LLZO) garnets are among the most promising solid electrolytes for next-generation batteries owing to their high ionic conductivity, chemical stability, and compatibility with lithium metal. Raman spectroscopy is commonly employed to distinguish the highly conductive cubic phase from the poorly conductive tetragonal phase of LLZO, yet the atomistic origin of these spectral differences and their direct connection to Li-ion transport remain unresolved. Here, we close this gap by comparing computed and experimental Raman spectra for the tetragonal, cubic, and Ta-doped variants of LLZO, with the computed spectra obtained from the MD-Raman approach that combines machine-learning molecular dynamics with first-principles polarizability calculations. We show that the contrasting ionic transport behavior across these LLZO variants is encoded in the vibrational dynamics of the lithium sublattice and gives rise to distinct features in their Raman spectra. A symmetry-resolved analysis further reveals that experimentally observed Raman peaks do not correspond to individual normal modes, but instead arise from overlapping contributions of multiple symmetry channels, challenging conventional peak-assignment approaches. By explicitly connecting experimentally accessible Raman signatures to the underlying atomic-scale dynamics, our results show how Raman spectroscopy can move beyond empirical phase identification toward a microscopic probe of Li-ion dynamics in lithium garnet electrolytes. △ Less

    Beyond Perturbation Magnitude: Direction-Dependent Responses in Multimodal Geometric Representations

    Authors: Yongsheng Luo, Wengan He, Yu Li, Rouying Wu, Wei Lv

    Abstract: …ranking accuracies of 0.864-0.963, and response-sign accuracies of 0.909-0.989, whereas the tested direction-free alternatives remain weak or unstable under the corresponding evaluation protocols. A pre-specified gain-normalization candidate, V/(g_V+eps), fails its predictability and clean-order gates. DGR uses the observed degraded-state displacement and is… ▽ More Geometric alignment scores based on Gram determinants provide a compact way to model higher-order consistency among modalities, yet how such scores respond to modality degradation is poorly understood. This paper asks whether the response of a multimodal geometric score is determined primarily by the magnitude of the perturbation-induced displacement. Using frozen cohorts from MSR-VTT (N=878) and DiDeMo (N=980), we apply controlled video blur and audio noise and analyze the response in the relational geometry on which the score is defined. Displacement magnitude explains at most 15% of the out-of-sample variance in the absolute response, and magnitude-matched pairs respond systematically differently, so scalar magnitude does not organize the response. The closed-form first-order expansion of the Gramian volume yields the Directional Geometric Response (DGR): the projection of the displacement onto the local volume gradient, which jointly captures the clean operating point, displacement magnitude, and displacement direction. The absolute first-order DGR term explains the observed response with out-of-sample R^2 of 0.838-0.969, matched-magnitude ranking accuracies of 0.864-0.963, and response-sign accuracies of 0.909-0.989, whereas the tested direction-free alternatives remain weak or unstable under the corresponding evaluation protocols. A pre-specified gain-normalization candidate, V/(g_V+eps), fails its predictability and clean-order gates. DGR uses the observed degraded-state displacement and is therefore an explanatory quantity, not a deployment-time predictor: geometric response depends on where the representation operates, how far degradation moves the relational geometry, and in which direction it moves. △ Less

The influence of altitude-dependent turbulence anisotropy on beam evolution is assessed through changes in intensity distribution and degradation of spatial structure. Polarization characteristics of the propagated fields exhibiting C-point and V-point polarization singularities are quantified using the Stokes parameter framework, from which the degree of polarization is derived based on ensemble-averaged electric field correlations. To further interpret polarization dynamics, the corresponding Stokes vectors are mapped onto the higher order Poincaré sphere, providing a geometric visualization of turbulence-driven depolarization and polarization state dispersion. Numerical simulations have shown that the anisotropy of turbulence significantly enhances the polarization instability. Horizontal and slant-path propagations suffer stronger depolarization and polarization-state diffusion than vertical propagation. These results demonstrate the importance of vectorial polarization analysis in describing the propagation of a structured beam in real atmospheric environments. Overall, C-point polarization singularities of circular Airy vector beams are found to be the most resistant to turbulence-induced intensity fluctuation and polarization degradation in anisotropic atmospheric conditions. △ Less

    Illumination-Aware Active Perception for Spacecraft Inspection

    Authors: Sagarika Rao Valluri, Benjamin Riviere

    Abstract: …overlook illumination effects including eclipse, self-shadowing, and specular reflection from spacecraft materials and tend to declare components as inspected despite the corresponding images lacking sufficient information. We propose an inspection-planning framework that replaces geometric visibility with a learned measure of camera information derived from… ▽ More Harsh and rapidly varying orbital illumination introduces failure modes during spacecraft inspection. Existing planners rely on line-of-sight inspection metrics which perform occlusion checks against a known target and conflate geometric visibility with task-relevant information. These metrics overlook illumination effects including eclipse, self-shadowing, and specular reflection from spacecraft materials and tend to declare components as inspected despite the corresponding images lacking sufficient information. We propose an inspection-planning framework that replaces geometric visibility with a learned measure of camera information derived from a classifier variational autoencoder (VAE) that operates directly on the captured image. The encoder model is trained to classify spacecraft surface components from rendered patches, and its total latent variance is used to learn the varying illumination of said components, replacing binary visibility checks with a continuous, image-grounded quantity. We evaluate the approach by implementing a Guidance, Navigation, and Control architecture in a photorealistic physics simulator, and vary lighting and viewing geometries with multiple orbits. We show that, although all approaches successfully maximize their own objective, our approach results in better illumination: a mean brightness of $0.57$, with $81.6\%$ of observations above $0.25$, compared with $0.02$ ($3.0\%$) for geometric planning with an illumination band and $0.04$ ($6.0\%$) for purely geometric planning. △ Less

    Quasinormal modes of thick branes in $f(R,T)$ gravity

    Authors: Yu-Peng E, Hai-Long Jia, Chun-Chun Zhu

    Abstract: …gravity. Starting from the background thick brane solutions, we derive the Schrödinger-like equation for gravitational perturbations and construct the corresponding effective potential. The effective potential exhibits a double-barrier structure whose properties depend on the model parameters. We analyze transmission spectra and find good agreement between… ▽ More We study tensor perturbations and quasinormal modes (QNMs) of thick branes in $f(R,T)=R+αT$ gravity. Starting from the background thick brane solutions, we derive the Schrödinger-like equation for gravitational perturbations and construct the corresponding effective potential. The effective potential exhibits a double-barrier structure whose properties depend on the model parameters. We analyze transmission spectra and find good agreement between resonance peaks and the real parts of the quasinormal frequencies. The parameters $b$ and $α$ mainly control the width and height of the potential barriers, respectively. QNMs are extracted using both time-domain evolution and direct integration methods, showing consistent results for low-lying modes. Higher overtones become increasingly difficult to extract due to stronger damping and numerical contamination. Our results indicate that $f(R,T)$ corrections significantly affect both the effective potential and the quasinormal spectrum, providing a possible imprint of modified gravity in braneworld perturbations. △ Less

    Test-Time Prompt-Agnostic Decomposition

    Authors: Junze Wang, Lei Fan, Dezheng Zhang, Donglin Di, Yang Song, Sidong Liu, Cong Cong

    Abstract: …Existing TPT methods are effective, but they mainly optimize each prompt update locally. Across test-time steps, prompts updated at the current step are reused for the corresponding predictions and updates, so noisy or biased unlabeled objectives can accumulate and destabilize the prompt-update trajectory. We identify two failure modes: magnitude expansion,… ▽ More Test-Time Prompt Tuning (TPT) adapts large pretrained models to unseen target distribution shifts by updating only lightweight prompt tokens. Existing TPT methods are effective, but they mainly optimize each prompt update locally. Across test-time steps, prompts updated at the current step are reused for the corresponding predictions and updates, so noisy or biased unlabeled objectives can accumulate and destabilize the prompt-update trajectory. We identify two failure modes: magnitude expansion, where updates move the prompt too far, and directional drift, where consecutive updates point in inconsistent directions. To analyze and control these failures, we propose Test-Time Prompt-Agnostic Decomposition ($\mathtt{TPD}$), which characterizes observed prompt-update trajectories from a dynamical-systems view and decomposes base prompt updates into magnitude and direction. $\mathtt{TPD}$ computes a spectral radius to measure update expansion and decomposes update directions into persistent, oscillatory, and residual components. Building on this decomposition, Adaptive Koopman Control ($\mathtt{AKC}$) regulates update magnitude by shrinking updates under expansive recent dynamics, while Hankel Update Router ($\mathtt{HUR}$) refines update direction by preserving persistent components and suppressing oscillatory and residual components. Together, $\mathtt{AKC}$ and $\mathtt{HUR}$ produce a stabilized prompt update. As a prompt-agnostic framework, $\mathtt{TPD}$ can be plugged into visual and text TPT methods without modifying the backbone, prompt architecture, or adaptation loss. Experiments on 15 datasets show that $\mathtt{TPD}$ consistently improves 10 baselines, with accuracy gains of $3$--$8\%$ for visual prompts and over $2\%$ for text prompts. △ Less

    Surface Reconstruction from Range Scans Under Visibility Constraints

    Authors: Gabriel Lenain, Edmond Boyer, Jean Ponce

    Abstract: …of noise and outliers, as fidelity to observations must be balanced against sensitivity to measurement errors. Yet, most neural approaches discard the sensor positions and corresponding visibility information, compensating for this loss with geometric priors or regularization ter… ▽ More Surface reconstruction from range scans is challenging in the presence of noise and outliers, as fidelity to observations must be balanced against sensitivity to measurement errors. Yet, most neural approaches discard the sensor positions and corresponding visibility information, compensating for this loss with geometric priors or regularization terms. We instead formulate surface reconstruction as robust regression of the predicted first surface intersection along each acquisition ray against its measured depth, which penalizes predicted surface intersections within the segment between the sensor and the observed point. We represent the surface as the zero set of a 1-Lipschitz multi-layer perceptron, allowing the use of linearly convergent sphere tracing to compute the first hit, and use a robust Huber loss for regression. Our formulation requires neither oriented normals, sampled off-surface points, nor an eikonal loss. Comparisons with the state of the art on synthetic and real data with varying levels of noise and outlier contamination demonstrate competitive performance on clean data and much improved fidelity and robustness under even moderate corruption. Our analysis further confirms a trade-off between fidelity and robustness: greater fitting capacity improves data fidelity in the clean case but increases sensitivity to the measurement errors inherent in real acquisitions, with a sweet spot typically emerging at intermediate capacity. △ Less

Existing approaches, however, often model appearance, metric geometry represented by projected LiDAR depth, and ego motion either partially or independently, overlooking the fact that these modalities are different projections of a shared underlying world state. This separation can lead to information loss and cross-modal inconsistency, ultimately limiting downstream performance. We freeze DriveUniTok and adapt a pretrained diffusion model, DriveUniWAM, to predict future unified latents; the tokenizer then decodes them into future appearance, projected LiDAR depth, and ego trajectories. Experiments on NAVSIM demonstrate that our unified design achieves better future prediction and planning performance at lower downstream computational cost than separate-stream alternatives.     Authors: Christophe Cheverry, Rida Harb, St{é}phane Junca

    Abstract: …Otherwise, they are damped through shock formation. In the baseline supercritical regime, we give an explicit criterion distinguishing these two behaviors and justify the corresponding asymptotic descriptions. Under… ▽ More This article studies the propagation, near a smooth non-constant ground state, of monophase oscillatory wave trains with small amplitude and very high frequency for multidimensional nonlinear scalar conservation laws. These oscillations lie beyond the classical regime of uniformly bounded variation. Two scenarios may occur. Under suitable compatibility conditions, the oscillations propagate. Otherwise, they are damped through shock formation. In the baseline supercritical regime, we give an explicit criterion distinguishing these two behaviors and justify the corresponding asymptotic descriptions. At higher frequencies, the propagation mechanism becomes more restrictive and is governed by compatibility conditions involving a Lie algebra. Under a natural local constant-rank assumption, we obtain a necessary and sufficient condition for the existence of complete WKB expansions. We also prove cancellation under first-order incompatibility. Perturbing a non-constant background state limits the propagation of small scales compared with the constant-background case. Our constructions contribute to the understanding of the fractional smoothing effect conjectured by Lions, Perthame and Tadmor. △ Less

By taking the late-time limit, we identify two late-time operators with scaling dimensions $Δ=1$ (leading) and $Δ=2$ (subleading). We introduce CFT-inspired inner products invariant under the dS group ($SO(4,1)$) for states created by late-time operators acting on the Bunch-Davies vacuum. We also explain how the corresponding discrete series representations of $SO(4,1)$ split into a direct sum of representations corresponding to two helicities $+1$ and $-1$. This is achieved by taking advantage of the dS invariance of the self-dual and anti-self-dual sectors of the photon field strength. In our outlook, we draw inspiration from a recent proposal for the microscopic description of $dS_4$ higher-spin gravity where conformal gauge fields in 3 dimensions play a central role, and we investigate a possible connection of the $Δ=1$ late-time operator with a conformal spin-1 gauge field in 3 dimensions. △ Less

Collaborative dense SLAM systems typically resolve this with depth sensors, which add payload, power, and calibration cost. We present CoMo3R-SLAM, a collaborative monocular dense SLAM system that places learned feed-forward 3D reconstruction priors at the center of the multi-agent problem: their dense pointmaps anchor scale across agents and supply correspondences strong enough to verify inter-agent links geometrically. Each agent tracks and fuses its own keyframes from a single RGB stream, while a coordinator retrieves cross-agent keyframes over the prior's encoder features, verifies them by bidirectional dense pointmap matching, synchronizes the independent similarity gauges in closed form, and refines every keyframe in one unified multi-agent sim(3) graph. Finally, a pose-depth alternation over geometry-aware segments lets inter-agent observations constrain dense structure as well as trajectories. Requiring neither measured depth nor supplied intrinsics, CoMo3R-SLAM attains the lowest trajectory error on three of four Tanks and Temples scenes, and competitive accuracy on Waymo driving sequences, while running at approximately 6-8 FPS on RTX 3080 Ti. A long-horizon traversal, independently captured day and night streams, and teams of up to four agents further map its operating range. △ Less

In previous work, temperature was probed via the vibrational eigenfrequency of a suspended single-layer SiNx nanofilm. However, the turning point of the frequency-temperature curve limited the usable measurement range. Moreover, the slow thermal response confined measurements to a stepwise protocol with only discrete temperature points. Here, we show that these limitations can be overcome with a suspended bilayer SiNx/Al nanofilm, whose temperature-dependent vibrational eigenfrequency provides a monotonic, continuous, and repeatable temperature readout. The frequency-temperature dependence is governed by the mismatch between the effective coefficients of thermal expansion (CTE) of the bilayer and the substrate, which makes the turning point tunable through the thickness ratio of the two layers. Over the 90 K to 300 K range, we characterize three bilayer nanofilms with different Al layer thicknesses and show how the turning point shifts with the thickness of the Al layer. For the sample with 72 nm Al, the turning point is pushed beyond 300 K, yielding a monotonic negative temperature coefficient over the entire 90 K to 300 K range. Combining a thin Al layer on the SiNx film with improved thermal anchoring of the chip shortens the thermal response to below 1 s, enabling continuous frequency tracking at a ramp rate of 3 K/min. We also demonstrate that the nanofilm with a 72 nm Al layer achieves a repeatability better than 150 mK above 175 K, and the frequency resolution of 1 Hz corresponds to a temperature resolution of ~5 mK. △ Less

These methods compress the stored context while approximately preserving attention computation, enabling generation with reduced memory consumption. Almost tight theoretical results on the worst case space complexity of attention have recently been obtained, but they are achieved by instances where queries can be perfectly aligned with keys. This directly contradicts the observed anisotropy of the key/query geometry in real transformers. In particular, it has been shown empirically that key/query cosine similarities are small even for most correlated pairs, i.e., near-perfect alignment of queries with keys does not happen in practice. In this paper, we study the space complexity of attention approximation under the assumption that key/query cosine similarities belong to a small interval around zero, i.e., a query cannot align too much with any key. We give a nearly space optimal algorithm and a corresponding space lower bound. Surprisingly, the distribution of key/query cosine similarities in our lower bound exhibits the anisotropy property empirically established and studied in the literature. Additionally, we show that the simple uniform sampling algorithm is nearly optimal for a typical range of parameters, supporting its surprising effectiveness on real datasets. △ Less

    Trivial topology-entailed states at interfaces between identical topological phases

    Authors: Julius Beck, Francesco S. Piccioli, Mark Kremer, Max Ehrhardt, Lukas J. Maczewsky, Nora Timm, Matthias Heinrich, Iacopo Carusotto, Alexander Szameit

    Abstract: …general interface phenomenon in composite topological systems and provide a unified framework for understanding hybridized boundary modes beyond conventional bulk-boundary correspondence. ▽ More Topological edge states arise at boundaries between bulk phases with distinct topological invariants and are characterized by robust, often chiral transport. When two domains sharing identical bulk topology are brought into contact, their counterpropagating edge modes are generally expected to hybridize and cancel each other out in the process. Here, we show that this cancellation can be lifted by trivial parameter variations at the interface, giving rise to a distinct class of gap-localized inter-face modes, which we term trivial topology-entailed (TTE) states. Using anomalous Floquet photonic lattices as a concrete platform, we experimentally observe the formation of bidirectional TTE states between topologically equivalent domains. In particular, we demonstrate their spectral confinement within bulk gaps, their coherent coupling to conventional chiral edge channels, and their ability to mediate interface-guided transport across composite lattices. Our results establish TTE states as a general interface phenomenon in composite topological systems and provide a unified framework for understanding hybridized boundary modes beyond conventional bulk-boundary correspondence. △ Less

    Real-Space Interference from Quantum Metric

    Authors: Bo-Qing Wu, Xing-Lei Ma, Jin-Xin Hu, Jinchao Zhao, K. T. Law

    Abstract: …Josephson STM the real-space interference can be experimentally observed through the tip-position dependence of the Josephson critical current. Our results reveal a direct correspondence between momentum-space quantum geometry and real-space interference, showing how bound-state profiles encode the momentum-resolved quantum metric. ▽ More Real-space interference of wave packets is governed by their central momentum separation and momentum width, which set the oscillation wavelength and spatial envelope, respectively. Here, we show that in crystalline systems, quantum metric distribution in an isolated band can similarly give rise to real-space interference through the evanescent tails of a localized bound state. The location and width of a quantum metric hot spot in the momentum space set a complex wavevector as one evanescent plane wave component of a bound state. Consequently, with multiple quantum metric hot spots, the density distribution of a bound state or the total density of two spatially overlapped bound states exhibits an interference pattern, where the oscillation period and long-range decay length are determined by the momentum-space separation of the hot spots and the width of the narrowest hot spots, respectively. This quantum-geometric interference is particularly pronounced in (nearly) flat bands, where competing scales from band dispersion are suppressed. Furthermore, we demonstrate that using a Josephson STM the real-space interference can be experimentally observed through the tip-position dependence of the Josephson critical current. Our results reveal a direct correspondence between momentum-space quantum geometry and real-space interference, showing how bound-state profiles encode the momentum-resolved quantum metric. △ Less

This yields explicit formulas refined by complete type and shows that the counts depend only on the relevant parameters, not on the particula… ▽ More Mass formulas for prescribed hull dimensions have mainly been studied for linear codes viewed as subspaces of the whole space. We study internal enumeration for an arbitrary fixed parent code. Let $C$ be an $[n,k]_q$ linear code with an $\ell$-dimensional hull, and let $s$ be the subcode dimension. We determine the number of subcodes with prescribed dimension, hull dimension, and type over finite fields of both even and odd characteristic. We decompose the parent code into an LCD complement and its hull, and use projection to reduce enumeration to the corresponding problem inside the LCD complement. This yields explicit formulas refined by complete type and shows that the counts depend only on the relevant parameters, not on the particular parent code or its length. The known whole-space mass formulas and the enumeration of LCD subcodes of LCD codes arise as special cases. Motivated by code extension, we also study the dual containment problem: for a fixed code, we enumerate larger codes containing it with prescribed hull dimension and type. By duality, this problem is converted into the subcode enumeration problem already solved above, leading to corresponding conditional mass formulas. Finally, we investigate the asymptotic distributions arising from the exact enumeration formulas. When both the subcode dimension $s$ and $k-\ell-s$ tend to infinity, the hull distribution is governed by the LCD complement, and the limiting proportions are explicit constants determined by the field, the prescribed hull dimension, and the relevant type. When $s$ approaches or exceeds the dimension $k-\ell$ of the LCD complement, we determine the asymptotic proportion of subcodes attaining the minimum possible hull dimension and establish the associated concentration behavior. Analogous asymptotic results are obtained for the containment problem. △ Less

    Authors: Lizhe Zhang, Wentao Chen, Li Zhong, Letian Peng, Zilong Wang, Jingbo Shang

    Abstract: …superficially similar but semantically different variants of existing coding tasks by modifying a core logic element of the reference solution and regenerating the corresponding prompt. To summarize this behavior, we introduce the \emph{Harmful Memorization Index} (HMI), an operational diagnostic that combines similarity to the original solution with perform… ▽ More Assessing memorization in LLM code generation is challenging because code reuse is not inherently harmful. Correct programs often share APIs, templates, and algorithmic idioms, so code similarity alone may reflect benign reuse rather than problematic memorization. We focus on the harmful case: a model fails to adapt when task semantics change and instead produces code that remains highly similar to the original solution. We propose \emph{code rewriting}, an automated semantic perturbation pipeline that constructs superficially similar but semantically different variants of existing coding tasks by modifying a core logic element of the reference solution and regenerating the corresponding prompt. To summarize this behavior, we introduce the \emph{Harmful Memorization Index} (HMI), an operational diagnostic that combines similarity to the original solution with performance degradation on rewritten tasks. We use harmful memorization as an operational behavioral term for this specific failure mode; HMI does not establish whether an original task or solution appeared in a model's training data. HMI is intended to complement standard accuracy metrics by capturing brittleness under small semantic changes. Across MBPP+ and BigCodeBench, we find that: (1) HMI does not monotonically increase with model scale and often decreases on simpler tasks; (2) high-HMI failures are concentrated in specific logic-edit types, especially constraint changes and data transformations; (3) supervised fine-tuning improves original-task accuracy but can substantially increase HMI on held-out tasks; and (4) across the evaluated post-training settings, PPO yields a more favorable accuracy--HMI trade-off than SFT. △ Less

    Decoherence of a quantum magneto-oscillator: Effect of field-bath interaction

    Authors: Koushik Mandal, Suraka Bhattacharjee, Supurna Sinha

    Abstract: …where the external confining potential modifies the random force correlations and the memory kernel of the environment, and the effective spring constant. Using the corresponding non-Markovian master equation, we examine the influence of the field-bath coupling on the temporal decay of the reduced density matrix of the system. We show that the additional in… ▽ More We investigate the quantum dissipative dynamics of a charged magneto-oscillator(CMO) coupled to a heat bath of harmonic oscillators in the presence of an additional field-bath interaction. The problem is formulated within the framework of the generalized quantum Langevin equation, where the external confining potential modifies the random force correlations and the memory kernel of the environment, and the effective spring constant. Using the corresponding non-Markovian master equation, we examine the influence of the field-bath coupling on the temporal decay of the reduced density matrix of the system. We show that the additional interaction leads to an enhancement of the decoherence rate by altering the dissipative response of the bath. Thus our results shed light on the role of field-bath coupling in the quantum to classical transition of a CMO. The resulting fluctuation-dissipation relation is analyzed, and the position autocorrelation, position-velocity correlation and velocity autocorrelation are obtained explicitly. The dependence of these experimentally accessible quantities on the field-bath coupling parameter is discussed. We outline an experimental proposal for testing our theoretical predictions. The study sheds light on reservoir engineering which is central to quantum technologies. △ Less

    MoRE: Motion-Regularized Encoding for Articulated 3D Generation

    Authors: Guantian Zheng, Shan Jin, Tim Tso, Hongfei Cai

    Abstract: …Plücker moment coefficients, removing the arbitrary choice of an origin along the axis. During representation learning, decoded joint hypotheses are used to move corresponding three-dimensional anchors, and their motions are compared with those of annotated joints. A differentiable expectation over joint types and axis directions couples attribute reconstruc… ▽ More Generating an articulated object from a single image requires recovering both its appearance and the mechanism governing its parts. A compact encoding of joint attributes does not by itself ensure that reconstruction preserves the resulting motion. We present MoRE, Motion-Regularized Encoding for articulated 3D generation. MoRE represents coordinate-aligned joint lines with axis classification and two axis-orthogonal Plücker moment coefficients, removing the arbitrary choice of an origin along the axis. During representation learning, decoded joint hypotheses are used to move corresponding three-dimensional anchors, and their motions are compared with those of annotated joints. A differentiable expectation over joint types and axis directions couples attribute reconstruction to the motion it induces. An image-conditioned flow learns the resulting functional latents, while a multi-state-adapted Gaussian model supplies appearance. At inference, recovered part assignments and joints articulate a single set of rest-pose Gaussians without regenerating the object for each state. Experiments on PartNet-Mobility and ACD show stronger part-box agreement, articulated-state geometry and visual similarity than the compared baselines, while joint prediction reduces axis, origin and range errors relative to ArtiLatent. Controlled ablations support the joint-line encoding and show that motion supervision reduces anchor-motion error. Code and pretrained checkpoints will be released in the future. △ Less

    Authors: Bo Zhang

    Abstract: …-coefficients. The comparison identifies the corresponding commutative coefficient algebras and is compatible with arbitrary pullback. Its special cases include rational coefficients over arbitrary bases, arbitra… ▽ More Let $S$ be a noetherian scheme of finite Krull dimension and let $R$ be a commutative ring in which every positive residue characteristic of $S$ is invertible. We prove that Voevodsky's stable Nisnevich category of motives with $R$-coefficients is canonically equivalent to modules over the zeroth slice of the motivic sphere with derived $R$-coefficients. The comparison identifies the corresponding commutative coefficient algebras and is compatible with arbitrary pullback. Its special cases include rational coefficients over arbitrary bases, arbitrary coefficients over $\mathbb Q$-schemes, and $\mathbb Z[1/p]$-algebra coefficients over $\mathbb Z_{(p)}$-schemes. The proof establishes base change for finite-cycle spectra using permutation quotients, families of cycles, and arithmetic power homotopies, then passes to general bases by universal homeomorphism invariance and continuity. △ Less

    Minimum attaining operators on reducing subspaces: Spectral structure and density

    Authors: Nag Puspendu, Golla Ramesh

    Abstract: …, obtain a corresponding characterization for multiplication operators on $L^2(\mathbb{T})$, and characterize the condition $\mathcal{R}_T=\mathcal{R}_{|T|}$ in terms of the von Neumann algebra generated by $|T|$, showing that this condition forces $T$ to be normal. ▽ More In this article, we introduce and study the class $\mathcal{M}_r(H)$ of minimum attaining operators on a separable Hilbert space $H$ whose restrictions to every nonzero reducing subspace are minimum attaining. This class lies between the classes of absolutely minimum attaining operators and minimum attaining operators. We establish a spectral characterization of positive operators in $\mathcal{M}_r(H)$: a positive operator $T$ belongs to $\mathcal{M}_r(H)$ if and only if $T=\sum_{λ\inΛ}λP_λ$, where $Λ\subseteq[0,\|T\|]$ is a countable well-ordered set, $(P_λ)_{λ\inΛ}$ is a family of mutually orthogonal nonzero projections, and $\sum_{λ\inΛ}P_λ=I$ in the strong operator topology. We also establish a connection between $\mathcal{M}_r(H)$ and the class of operators attaining their norm on every nonzero reducing subspace. Further, we obtain stability results under suitable finite-rank and compact perturbations, and prove that $\mathcal{M}_r(H)$ is norm dense in $\mathcal{B}(H)$. We also establish the norm density of operators in this class having a nontrivial invariant half-space. Finally, we characterize normal operators in $\mathcal{M}_r(H)$, obtain a corresponding characterization for multiplication operators on $L^2(\mathbb{T})$, and characterize the condition $\mathcal{R}_T=\mathcal{R}_{|T|}$ in terms of the von Neumann algebra generated by $|T|$, showing that this condition forces $T$ to be normal. △ Less

    SLVR: Structured Latent Visual Reasoning via Human-like Reasoning Flows

    Authors: Albert Gao, Bing Xue, Andrea Zanette

    Abstract: …visually plausible distractors. It then organizes reasoning into latent stages for planning, grounding, evidence selection, and integration, supervising each stage with the corresponding signal: plans, boxes, visual evidence, and final rationales. This gives latent reasoning an explicit functional structure while avoiding generated textual chains at inferenc… ▽ More Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) often answer visual reasoning questions by relying on linguistic priors rather than task-relevant visual evidence. Textual chain-of-thought reasoning can partially mitigate this issue by encouraging models to decompose visual questions into intermediate evidence-seeking steps, but generating these steps autoregressively increases inference cost. Latent reasoning avoids explicit rationale generation, but existing approaches provide limited control over what intermediate states encode, making it difficult to impose separate supervision for planning, grounding, and evidence selection. We propose Structured Latent Visual Reasoning (SLVR), a training framework that bridges explicit chain-of-thought and latent reasoning by organizing multimodal reasoning into typed latent stages for planning, grounding, evidence selection, and reasoning integration. SLVR first trains the model to rely on the image by masking answer-revealing text and contrasting the correct answer with visually plausible distractors. It then organizes reasoning into latent stages for planning, grounding, evidence selection, and integration, supervising each stage with the corresponding signal: plans, boxes, visual evidence, and final rationales. This gives latent reasoning an explicit functional structure while avoiding generated textual chains at inference time. Built on Qwen2.5-VL-7B, SLVR improves consistently across multimodal reasoning benchmarks, with absolute gains of +9.4 on MMVP and +14.2 on BLINK Relation, as well as improvements on V*, MathVista, and ChartQA. These results suggest that structured latent supervision can improve fine-grained visual reasoning without the decoding overhead of textual CoT. Project page is available \href{https://bogao-code.github.io/SLVR/}{here}. △ Less

These motions are usually interpreted by assuming parameterized gravitational potentials or specific perturbation models, thereby entangling direct kinematic information with model-dependent assumptions. Using Gaia DR3 astrometry, we analyze the Galactic longitudinal-velocity residuals of nearly four million red-giant-branch stars over $-4 < X_h < 4\, \mathrm{kpc}$ and $-6 < Y_h < 6\, \mathrm{kpc}$ in the heliocentric region. We construct an empirical axisymmetric baseline without introducing any dynamical model, and compute the power spectrum of the residual velocity field. The spectrum exhibits a broad peak at spatial frequency of $f \simeq 0.12 - 0.22\, \mathrm{kpc}^{-1}$, corresponding to a scale of around $5-8\, \mathrm{kpc}$. The residual amplitude is sensitive to the adopted distance scale: inverse-parallax distances $d_\varpi$ yield a peak of around $16\, \mathrm{km\, s^{-1}}$, while the Bailer-Jones geometric distances $r_{\mathrm{geo}}$ yield around $12\, \mathrm{km\, s^{-1}}$. Minimizing the low-frequency power favors a solar radial motion of $U_\odot=10.8\, \mathrm{km\, s^{-1}}$ based on $d_\varpi$ and $U_\odot=10.4\, \mathrm{km\, s^{-1}}$ based on $r_{\mathrm{geo}}$. Collectively, the resulting spectrum establishes Gaia astrometry as a direct probe of disk-wide non-axisymmetric kinematics and provides quantitative targets for future dynamical modeling and interpretation. △ Less

However, dynamically coordinating diverse search mechanisms as candidate quality, failure modes, and resource budgets evolve remains a critical open challenge. Targeted empirical diagnostics reveal that mechanism effectiveness is highly state-dependent. Motivated by this, we analyze how individual decisions drive final outcomes, decomposing the expected terminal improvement under a shared budget into cumulative decision opportunities minus cumulative selection losses. Guided by this opportunity-loss theoretical foundation, we propose OptiCom, a unified framework that represents LLM-driven optimizers within a shared configuration space: C=(A,Q,O,E,M,S), corresponding to artifact, query, operator, evaluation, memory, and strategy. Comprehensive evaluations across 32 benchmark groups demonstrate the superiority of framework: OptiCom achieves an average Max-score rank of 1.72 among 14 evaluated configurations, securing the top score in 23 groups. Ultimately, these results highlight the broad applicability and high extensibility of OptiCom as a general-purpose paradigm for robust LLM test-time scaling. △ Less

Our method is trained efficiently on two-view data, yet serves as a periodic refiner for streaming n-view pose estimators with no retraining.     Authors: Gyeonghun Kang, Pengxi Liu, Bingshuo Qian, Xiang Cheng

    Abstract: …even with unrestricted downstream computation, while the corresponding optimal risk for image prediction remains bounded. Without an input bottleneck, assuming exact affine recovery of the noisy patch from the model representation, we characteri… ▽ More Clean-image prediction ($x$-prediction) and noise prediction yield equivalent population-optimal denoisers, yet can perform very differently in finite diffusion networks. For noisy input $Y_t$ with noise standard deviation $σ_t$, a noise predictor $f_{\varepsilon,t}$ induces the clean-image estimate $\widehat X_1=Y_t-σ_t f_{\varepsilon,t}(Y_t)$. This reveals a noise-transmission burden: the predictor must preserve information needed to cancel the input corruption. Under Gaussian corruption of $d$-dimensional data, a linear input bottleneck of rank $r<d$ imposes a denoising-error floor $σ_t^2(d-r)$ even with unrestricted downstream computation, while the corresponding optimal risk for image prediction remains bounded. EDM attenuates this bottleneck contribution at high noise; exact preconditioning cancels it at every noise level. Without an input bottleneck, assuming exact affine recovery of the noisy patch from the model representation, we characterize how the covariance spectrum of corrections beyond linear denoising determines the width required above the patch dimension. ImageNet experiments with JiT show that measured denoising losses reflect the predicted bottleneck floor and its attenuation or cancellation through preconditioning. Further widening yields larger relative denoising gains at low noise, where corrections beyond linear denoising spread across more directions, consistent with our spectral characterization of the required model width. Together, these results provide a denoising-based account of practical generation-quality gaps across prediction targets, preconditioning choices, and model widths. △ Less

    Width and Depth Limits Commute During Training in Linear Residual Networks

    Authors: Ruijia Zhang, Soufiane Hayou

    Abstract: …width and depth tend to infinity. Consequently, the infinite-width and infinite-depth limits commute throughout any finite training horizon. Without gradient clipping, the corresponding $L^2$ convergence does not hold in general, but we establish the weaker convergence in probability. ▽ More Recent work established that the infinite-width and infinite-depth limits of residual networks commute at initialization when residual branches are scaled by $1/\sqrt{depth}$. In particular, the network output converges to the same limit whether width and depth tend to infinity sequentially or jointly. We extend this commutativity result to training in linear residual networks. Under the same $1/\sqrt{depth}$ residual-branch scaling (Depth-$μ$P) and the $μ$P width parameterization, we prove that, for any fixed number of gradient-descent steps with gradient clipping, the network output converges in $L^2$ along every joint width--depth growth path as both width and depth tend to infinity. Consequently, the infinite-width and infinite-depth limits commute throughout any finite training horizon. Without gradient clipping, the corresponding $L^2$ convergence does not hold in general, but we establish the weaker convergence in probability. △ Less

    On the Existence of Complete Coverings of Lee Spheres

    Authors: Jing Zhang, Yue Zhou

    Abstract: …from the codeword. We call such a configuration a complete covering. Under projection onto a suitable Cartesian product of cycles, a complete covering corresponds to an optimal local structure in the sense of Špacapan. The existence of a complete covering is necessary but, in general, not sufficient for the existence of a perfect Lee code, as our constructi… ▽ More Every perfect $r$-error-correcting Lee code in $\mathbb{Z}^n$ induces a finite configuration around each codeword: a family of pairwise disjoint radius-$r$ Lee spheres, all disjoint from the central sphere, that covers every lattice point at Lee distance $r+1$ from the codeword. We call such a configuration a complete covering. Under projection onto a suitable Cartesian product of cycles, a complete covering corresponds to an optimal local structure in the sense of Špacapan. The existence of a complete covering is necessary but, in general, not sufficient for the existence of a perfect Lee code, as our construction for $(n,r)=(3,2)$ shows. We prove that no complete covering exists whenever $n\ge 8$ and $r^2+\lfloor 25r/34\rfloor-3\ge 2n$. For the corresponding Cartesian products of cycles, this strengthens Špacapan's nonexistence theorem by replacing the linear radius condition $r\ge n$ with a threshold of order $\sqrt{2n}$. We also obtain an improved large-radius nonexistence bound for perfect Lee codes, strengthening the bound of Horak and Kim. Furthermore, we show that, for every $\varepsilon>0$, no complete covering exists when $n\ge 3$, $r$ is sufficiently large, and $r^2+(2/\mathrm{e}-\varepsilon)r\ge 2n$. On the positive side, we use near-optimal ternary constant-weight codes to construct almost complete coverings whose pairwise disjoint shadows cover a $1-o(1)$ proportion of the first outer Lee shell as the dimension tends to infinity, for every fixed radius. △ Less

    SNACK: A Sequential Notation Framework for Probabilistic Graph Generation

    Authors: Hohyun Kim, Hyesung Kim, Min-hwan Oh, Seunggeun Lee

    Abstract: …graphs with discrete node and edge features. SNACK linearizes graphs by encoding the nonzero entries of the lower-triangular adjacency matrix, establishing a bijective correspondence between valid sequences and ordered adjacency matrices. This design enables invalid logit masking to enforce structural constraints, such as chemical valence and ordering rules,… ▽ More We introduce SNACK, a string representation for probabilistic graph generation that enables language models to generate graphs with discrete node and edge features. SNACK linearizes graphs by encoding the nonzero entries of the lower-triangular adjacency matrix, establishing a bijective correspondence between valid sequences and ordered adjacency matrices. This design enables invalid logit masking to enforce structural constraints, such as chemical valence and ordering rules, thereby guaranteeing 100% valid generated sequences while sticking to standard language modeling for effective training. SNACK also enables principled string-based GFlowNet training by explicitly accounting for node-ordering symmetries. Across standard graph-generation tasks and molecular distribution-learning benchmarks, SNACK achieves competitive sample quality, while substantially improving the throughput of GFlowNet training relative to graph-based generation. Probing analyses further show that models trained on SNACK encode meaningful local and global graph structure despite receiving only sequential supervision. These results position SNACK as a practical framework for probabilistic graph generation with deep sequence models. △ Less
